# Tesbox Phase 0 — In-car Probe + Pipeline Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Ship a deployable probe page (`/probe/`) plus the build/Docker/CI pipeline, so the user can measure the Tesla browser's real behaviour (audio routes, steering-wheel events, GPS, headroom, storage) before the app depends on it.

**Architecture:** Vite 8 multi-page static site (app shell placeholder + probe page), vanilla TypeScript probe modules under `src/probe/`, shared platform helpers under `src/platform/`, an AudioWorklet probe processor, nginx-unprivileged container, GitHub Actions → GHCR.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 6.0, Vite 8.3, Vitest 5, Playwright 1.63, ESLint 10 + typescript-eslint 8, jsqr (tests), nginx-unprivileged:alpine, GitHub Actions.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-tesbox-design.md` (§3, §6.4, §12, §15 Phase 0, §16)

## Global Constraints

The locked contract `docs/superpowers/plans/contracts/phase0-contract.md` (including its **Amendments C1–C5**) and `src/contracts.ts` are part of every task's requirements. Key lines:

- Node ≥ 22.12; devDeps exactly as the contract's Toolchain section; no runtime dependencies.
- `vite.config.ts` imports `defineConfig` from `'vitest/config'`; local TS imports use `.ts` extensions; `build.rolldownOptions.input`; `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`.
- CSP from `deploy/csp.ts` is the single source; nginx and the Vite preview plugin must match it.
- No real coordinates, hostnames or IPs anywhere; README domain placeholder `https://tesbox.example.com`.
- No per-task commits — Phase 0 lands as one verified commit (pushed; approved exception).
- Each implementer works in the repo; any scratch work goes in its own unique scratch directory.

## Review Focus

1. Geolocation errors (denied / timeout) mid-run → the GPS run still resolves a summary with errors counted; stop() resolves promptly. (Task 5)
2. A reload during a GPS run leaves the crash marker → next load shows the banner once. (Task 5)
3. Start pressed twice / routes switched rapidly → exactly one AudioContext and one connected route. (Task 7)
4. Summary larger than one QR → chunked QRs each decode and reassemble to the original. (Task 3)
5. localStorage / Cache Storage throwing → probe still loads; storage report shows false + error. (Tasks 2, 9)

## Execution order (dependency waves)

W1: Task 1 → W2: Tasks 2, 3, 4 (Part A: log), 6, 11 → W3: Tasks 5, 7, 8, 9 → W4: Task 4 (Part B: summary) → W5: Task 10 → W6: Task 12 (e2e, CI, docs, full verification).

---

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from other tasks. `src/contracts.ts` already exists and is not modified.
- Produces:
  - `deploy/build-sha.ts`: `export function resolveBuildSha(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string` — `env.GITHUB_SHA` trimmed, first 7 chars (empty/blank counts as unset) → `git rev-parse --short HEAD` via `execSync` with stderr ignored → `'dev'`.
  - `deploy/version-plugin.ts`: `export function makeVersionJson(sha: string, builtAt: string): string` (returns `{"sha":"…","builtAt":"…"}` plus `\n`); `export function versionPlugin(sha: string): Plugin` (`import type { Plugin } from 'vite'`; `name: 'tesbox-version'`, `apply: 'build'`, emits asset `version.json` from `generateBundle`).
  - `src/platform/build-info.ts`: `export const BUILD_SHA: string`; `export interface VersionCheck { local: string; remote: string | null; stale: boolean; error?: string }`; `export function checkVersion(fetchFn?: typeof fetch, url?: string): Promise<VersionCheck>` — never rejects; default `url` = `new URL('/version.json', location.origin).href`; request init exactly `{ cache: 'no-store' }`; `stale = remote !== null && remote !== local`; non-2xx → `error: 'HTTP <status>'`; body without a non-empty string `sha` → `error: 'version.json has no "sha" string'`; network/parse failure → `remote: null, stale: false, error: <message>`.
  - `src/env.d.ts`: global `declare const __BUILD_SHA__: string;` + `/// <reference types="vite/client" />`.
  - `src/types/audioworklet.d.ts` (global script — no imports/exports): `declare class AudioWorkletProcessor { readonly port: MessagePort; constructor(options?: AudioWorkletNodeOptions) }`; `interface AudioWorkletProcessorImpl extends AudioWorkletProcessor { process(inputs: Float32Array[][], outputs: Float32Array[][], parameters: Record<string, Float32Array>): boolean }`; `type AudioWorkletProcessorConstructor = new (options: AudioWorkletNodeOptions) => AudioWorkletProcessorImpl`; `declare function registerProcessor(name: string, processorCtor: AudioWorkletProcessorConstructor): void`; `declare const currentTime: number`; `declare const sampleRate: number`.
  - `vite.config.ts` (default export): `define.__BUILD_SHA__`, `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`, `build.modulePreload: { polyfill: false }` (no Vite polyfill shipped; T12's NOTICES relies on it), `build.rolldownOptions.input = { main: <abs>/index.html, probe: <abs>/probe/index.html }`, `build.rolldownOptions.output.comments: { legal: true }` (keeps `/*!` / `@license` comments — T3's MIT attribution in `src/platform/qr.ts` — in the minified bundle; Vite 8.3 drops them by default when minifying), `plugins: [versionPlugin(sha)]`, `test: { include: ['tests/unit/**/*.test.ts'], environment: 'node' }`.
  - `index.html`: `div#unsupported[role=alert]` visible by default, `main#app`, one module script `/src/main.ts`; no inline script or style (CSP `script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'`).
  - `src/main.ts`: sets `#unsupported.hidden = true`; fills `#app` with `p#status` whose text is `Tesbox — under construction. Open /probe/` (`/probe/` is a link to `/probe/`), `p#build-sha` whose text is `Build <BUILD_SHA>`, and a footer `Not affiliated with Tesla, Inc.`
  - npm scripts exactly as the Phase 0 contract lists them.

**Cross-task premises:** (open the named file and confirm each premise before coding, if that task has already landed)
- `src/contracts.ts` exists and stays untouched by this task.
- T10 creates `probe/index.html`. Until it exists, `npm run build` fails with `[UNRESOLVED_ENTRY] Cannot resolve entry module probe/index.html` — expected. Step 22 builds with a temporary stub that it deletes again. Any task that runs `npm run build` must run after T10.
- T11 adds `headersPlugin()` to the `plugins` array of `vite.config.ts`; this task must not import `deploy/headers-plugin.ts`. T11 should import it with the `.ts` extension like the two existing config imports — Vite 8.3 prints `(!) Your Vite config uses features that are unsupported by configLoader: 'native'` for extensionless imports in the config; `allowImportingTsExtensions` in `tsconfig.json` makes the `.ts` form type-check.
- T12's `tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts` relies on: `#unsupported` gets the `hidden` attribute after boot; the SHA is shown as text `Build <sha>` inside `#build-sha` and equals the `sha` in `/version.json` of the same build.
- T6's `src/probe/audio-worklet.ts` uses the ambient globals from `src/types/audioworklet.d.ts` (`class … extends AudioWorkletProcessor { process(…): boolean }`, `registerProcessor('tesbox-probe', …)`, `currentTime`, `sampleRate`). They are visible to the whole program; main-thread code must not use them.
- Phase 2a T10 appends a config object to the `defineConfig(...)` argument list in `eslint.config.js`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `tsconfig.json`**

```json
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2022",
    "module": "ESNext",
    "moduleResolution": "Bundler",
    "lib": ["ES2022", "DOM", "DOM.Iterable"],
    "types": ["vite/client", "node"],
    "strict": true,

```

Every contract option is present. `allowImportingTsExtensions` (legal because `noEmit` is set) is
added so `vite.config.ts` can import `./deploy/*.ts` with explicit extensions, which Vite 8.3
requires to stay warning-free (see premises). No `skipLibCheck`: the whole program type-checks
cleanly with these packages.

- [ ] **Step 6: Create the ambient declarations `src/env.d.ts` and `src/types/audioworklet.d.ts`**

`src/env.d.ts`:

```ts
/// <reference types="vite/client" />

/** Short git SHA of this build, injected by `define` in vite.config.ts. */
declare const __BUILD_SHA__: string;
```

`src/types/audioworklet.d.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * Ambient globals of AudioWorkletGlobalScope (the TypeScript DOM lib does not ship them).
 * They are visible to the whole program; only code that runs inside an AudioWorklet module
 * may touch them at runtime.
 */

/** Base class every processor extends; `port` talks to the AudioWorkletNode on the main thread. */
declare class AudioWorkletProcessor {
  readonly port: MessagePort;
  constructor(options?: AudioWorkletNodeOptions);
}

/** What `registerProcessor` requires of a processor instance. */
interface AudioWorkletProcessorImpl extends AudioWorkletProcessor {
  process(
    inputs: Float32Array[][],
    outputs: Float32Array[][],
    parameters: Record<string, Float32Array>,
  ): boolean;
}

type AudioWorkletProcessorConstructor = new (
  options: AudioWorkletNodeOptions,
) => AudioWorkletProcessorImpl;

declare function registerProcessor(
  name: string,
  processorCtor: AudioWorkletProcessorConstructor,
): void;

/** Context time in seconds of the current render quantum. */
declare const currentTime: number;
/** Sample rate of the owning BaseAudioContext, in Hz. */
declare const sampleRate: number;
```

Both files must stay free of `import`/`export` so their declarations are global. `declare class`
(rather than `declare var`) keeps `class P extends AudioWorkletProcessor` and lint's `no-var`
happy; the TypeScript DOM lib has `AudioWorkletNodeOptions` but none of these scope globals.

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the failing test `tests/unit/build-sha.test.ts`**

```ts
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';

vi.mock('node:child_process', () => ({ execSync: vi.fn() }));

import { execSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { resolveBuildSha } from '../../deploy/build-sha';

const execMock = vi.mocked(execSync);

describe('resolveBuildSha', () => {
  afterEach(() => {
    execMock.mockReset();
  });

  it('uses the first 7 characters of GITHUB_SHA without calling git', () => {
    expect(resolveBuildSha({ GITHUB_SHA: '0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567' })).toBe(
      '0123456',
    );
    expect(execMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it('falls back to git rev-parse with stderr ignored when GITHUB_SHA is unset', () => {
    execMock.mockReturnValue('abc1234\n');
    expect(resolveBuildSha({})).toBe('abc1234');
    expect(execMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(execMock).toHaveBeenCalledWith('git rev-parse --short HEAD', {
      encoding: 'utf8',
      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'],
    });
  });

  it('treats an empty or blank GITHUB_SHA as unset', () => {
    execMock.mockReturnValue('fedcba9\n');
    expect(resolveBuildSha({ GITHUB_SHA: '' })).toBe('fedcba9');
    expect(resolveBuildSha({ GITHUB_SHA: '   ' })).toBe('fedcba9');
  });

  it("returns 'dev' when git fails", () => {
    execMock.mockImplementation(() => {
      throw new Error('fatal: not a git repository');
    });
    expect(resolveBuildSha({})).toBe('dev');
  });

  it("returns 'dev' when git prints nothing", () => {
    execMock.mockReturnValue('   \n');
    expect(resolveBuildSha({})).toBe('dev');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Implement `deploy/build-sha.ts`**

```ts
import { execSync } from 'node:child_process';

/**
 * Short build SHA: GITHUB_SHA (CI / Docker build-arg) → `git rev-parse --short HEAD` → 'dev'.
 * An empty GITHUB_SHA (Dockerfile `ENV GITHUB_SHA=$GITHUB_SHA` without the build-arg) counts as
 * unset.
 */
export function resolveBuildSha(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
  const fromEnv = env.GITHUB_SHA?.trim().slice(0, 7);
  if (fromEnv) return fromEnv;
  try {
    const out = execSync('git rev-parse --short HEAD', {
      encoding: 'utf8',
      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'],
    });
    const sha = String(out).trim();
    if (sha) return sha;
  } catch {
    // Not a git checkout (Docker build context has no .git) or no commits yet.
  }
  return 'dev';
}
```

```ts
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { makeVersionJson, versionPlugin } from '../../deploy/version-plugin';

describe('makeVersionJson', () => {
  it('serialises sha and builtAt as one JSON line', () => {
    const text = makeVersionJson('abc1234', '2026-01-02T03:04:05.000Z');
    expect(text).toBe('{"sha":"abc1234","builtAt":"2026-01-02T03:04:05.000Z"}\n');
    expect(JSON.parse(text)).toEqual({ sha: 'abc1234', builtAt: '2026-01-02T03:04:05.000Z' });
  });
});

describe('versionPlugin', () => {
  afterEach(() => {
    vi.useRealTimers();
  });

  it('is a build-only plugin', () => {
    const plugin = versionPlugin('abc1234');
    expect(plugin.name).toBe('tesbox-version');
    expect(plugin.apply).toBe('build');
  });

  it('emits version.json as an asset in generateBundle', () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    vi.setSystemTime(new Date('2026-01-02T03:04:05.000Z'));
    const emitted: unknown[] = [];
    const context = {
      emitFile(file: unknown): string {
        emitted.push(file);
        return 'ref';
      },
    };
    const hook = versionPlugin('abc1234').generateBundle;
    const fn = typeof hook === 'function' ? hook : hook?.handler;
    if (!fn) throw new Error('versionPlugin has no generateBundle hook');
    fn.call(context as never, {} as never, {} as never, false);
    expect(emitted).toEqual([
      {
        type: 'asset',
        fileName: 'version.json',
        source: makeVersionJson('abc1234', '2026-01-02T03:04:05.000Z'),
      },
    ]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 13: Implement `deploy/version-plugin.ts`**

```ts
import type { Plugin } from 'vite';

/** Body of dist/version.json. Pure, so the plugin and the unit test share it. */
export function makeVersionJson(sha: string, builtAt: string): string {
  return `${JSON.stringify({ sha, builtAt })}\n`;
}

/** Emits `version.json` ({"sha","builtAt"}) at the root of the build output. */
export function versionPlugin(sha: string): Plugin {
  return {
    name: 'tesbox-version',
    apply: 'build',
    generateBundle() {
      this.emitFile({
        type: 'asset',
        fileName: 'version.json',
        source: makeVersionJson(sha, new Date().toISOString()),
      });
    },
  };
}
```

`generateBundle` runs once per build (the multi-page build is a single bundle), so exactly one
`dist/version.json` is written. `apply: 'build'` keeps it out of the dev server and Vitest.

- [ ] **Step 15: Create `vite.config.ts`**

```ts
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';
import { resolveBuildSha } from './deploy/build-sha.ts';
import { versionPlugin } from './deploy/version-plugin.ts';

const sha = resolveBuildSha();

/** Absolute path of a page next to this config, so builds work from any working directory. */
const page = (rel: string): string => fileURLToPath(new URL(rel, import.meta.url));

export default defineConfig({
  define: { __BUILD_SHA__: JSON.stringify(sha) },
  build: {
    // Every asset stays a real file (spec §4.1): nothing is inlined as a data: URI.
    assetsInlineLimit: 0,
    // Chromium 140+ supports <link rel=modulepreload> natively; without this Vite injects its
    // MIT-licensed polyfill into the entry chunk, and NOTICES / CLEAN_ROOM list the attributed QR
    // encoder adaptation (src/platform/qr.ts) as the only third-party code that ships.
    modulePreload: { polyfill: false },
    rolldownOptions: {
      input: { main: page('./index.html'), probe: page('./probe/index.html') },
      // Keep `/*!` / `@license` comments (the MIT attribution of the QR encoder adapted in
      // src/platform/qr.ts) in the minified bundle; Vite 8.3 strips them by default when minifying.
      output: { comments: { legal: true } },
    },
  },
  plugins: [versionPlugin(sha)],
  test: {
    include: ['tests/unit/**/*.test.ts'],
    environment: 'node',
  },
});
```

Notes:
- `build.rolldownOptions` is the Vite 8 name; `build.rollupOptions` is typed `@deprecated` in
  Vite 8.3 (“Use `rolldownOptions` instead”). If a future Vite rejected `rolldownOptions`, the
  build would fail loudly at Step 22 — then switch the key back to `rollupOptions`.
- `defineConfig` comes from `vitest/config` so the `test` block is typed; the `define` value also
  applies inside Vitest, which is what lets `build-info.test.ts` read `BUILD_SHA`.
- Inputs are absolute so `vite build` works from any working directory.
- `output.comments.legal`: Vite 8.3 sets `comments.legal = !minify`, so a production build drops
  even `/*! … @license … */` comments. With `{ legal: true }` (only `legal` is overridden; JSDoc and
  other comments are still stripped) T3's MIT attribution stays in `dist/assets/probe-*.js` (checked
  with Vite 8.3.3: 0 occurrences of `Nayuki` without it, the full header with it). Phase 0 ships no
  other third-party code, so no other legal comments appear.

- [ ] **Step 16: Write the failing test `tests/unit/build-info.test.ts`**

```ts
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { resolveBuildSha } from '../../deploy/build-sha';
import { BUILD_SHA, checkVersion } from '../../src/platform/build-info';

const VERSION_URL = 'https://tesbox.example.com/version.json';

function fakeFetch(respond: () => Promise<Response>) {
  const calls: { url: string; init: RequestInit | undefined }[] = [];
  const fn = (input: RequestInfo | URL, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> => {
    calls.push({ url: String(input), init });
    return respond();
  };
  return { fetchFn: fn as typeof fetch, calls };
}

const json = (body: unknown, status = 200): Promise<Response> =>
  Promise.resolve(new Response(JSON.stringify(body), { status }));

describe('BUILD_SHA', () => {
  it('is the SHA vite.config.ts injected via define', () => {
    expect(BUILD_SHA).toBe(resolveBuildSha());
    expect(BUILD_SHA.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });
});

describe('checkVersion', () => {
  afterEach(() => {
    vi.unstubAllGlobals();
  });

  it('fetches the url with cache no-store and reports up to date', async () => {
    const { fetchFn, calls } = fakeFetch(() => json({ sha: BUILD_SHA, builtAt: 'x' }));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(calls).toEqual([{ url: VERSION_URL, init: { cache: 'no-store' } }]);
    expect(result).toEqual({ local: BUILD_SHA, remote: BUILD_SHA, stale: false });
  });

  it('reports stale when the deployed sha differs', async () => {
    const { fetchFn } = fakeFetch(() => json({ sha: 'zzzzzzz', builtAt: 'x' }));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(result).toEqual({ local: BUILD_SHA, remote: 'zzzzzzz', stale: true });
  });

  it('reports an HTTP error without throwing', async () => {
    const { fetchFn } = fakeFetch(() => json({ error: 'nope' }, 404));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(result).toEqual({ local: BUILD_SHA, remote: null, stale: false, error: 'HTTP 404' });
  });

  it('reports a network failure without throwing', async () => {
    const { fetchFn } = fakeFetch(() => Promise.reject(new TypeError('Failed to fetch')));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(result).toEqual({
      local: BUILD_SHA,
      remote: null,
      stale: false,
      error: 'Failed to fetch',
    });
  });

  it('reports unparseable JSON without throwing', async () => {
    const { fetchFn } = fakeFetch(() => Promise.resolve(new Response('<html>', { status: 200 })));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(result.local).toBe(BUILD_SHA);
    expect(result.remote).toBeNull();
    expect(result.stale).toBe(false);
    expect(typeof result.error).toBe('string');
  });

  it('rejects a body without a sha string', async () => {
    const { fetchFn } = fakeFetch(() => json({ sha: 42 }));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(result).toEqual({
      local: BUILD_SHA,
      remote: null,
      stale: false,
      error: 'version.json has no "sha" string',
    });
  });

  it('rejects an empty sha string', async () => {
    const { fetchFn } = fakeFetch(() => json({ sha: '' }));
    const result = await checkVersion(fetchFn, VERSION_URL);
    expect(result).toEqual({ local: BUILD_SHA, remote: null, stale: false, error: 'version.json has no "sha" string' });
  });

  it('defaults to /version.json on the page origin', async () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('location', { origin: 'https://tesbox.example.com' });
    const { fetchFn, calls } = fakeFetch(() => json({ sha: BUILD_SHA }));
    await checkVersion(fetchFn);
    expect(calls[0]?.url).toBe('https://tesbox.example.com/version.json');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 18: Implement `src/platform/build-info.ts`**

```ts
/** Short SHA of the running build (replaced at build time by Vite `define`). */
export const BUILD_SHA: string = __BUILD_SHA__;

export interface VersionCheck {
  local: string;
  remote: string | null;
  stale: boolean;
  error?: string;
}

/**
 * Compares the running build with the deployed one via `version.json`, fetched with
 * `cache: 'no-store'` because the car's browser caches HTML indefinitely. Never throws.
 */
export async function checkVersion(
  fetchFn: typeof fetch = (input, init) => fetch(input, init),
  url: string = new URL('/version.json', location.origin).href,
): Promise<VersionCheck> {
  const local = BUILD_SHA;
  try {
    const res = await fetchFn(url, { cache: 'no-store' });
    if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);
    const body: unknown = await res.json();
    const remote =
      typeof body === 'object' && body !== null && 'sha' in body ? body.sha : undefined;
    if (typeof remote !== 'string' || remote === '') {
      throw new Error('version.json has no "sha" string');
    }
    return { local, remote, stale: remote !== local };
  } catch (err) {
    return {
      local,
      remote: null,
      stale: false,
      error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
    };
  }
}
```

The default `fetchFn` wraps the global `fetch` in an arrow so it is never invoked with a foreign
`this` (avoids “Illegal invocation”). The default `url` is evaluated only when the caller omits it,
so Node tests that pass a URL never touch `location`.

```html
<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
  <head>
    <meta charset="utf-8" />
    <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1" />
    <meta name="color-scheme" content="dark" />
    <link rel="icon" href="data:," />
    <title>Tesbox</title>
  </head>
  <body>
    <!-- Visible by default: browsers without ES modules (MCU1) never run the bundle that hides it. -->
    <div id="unsupported" role="alert">
      <h1>Tesbox can't run in this browser</h1>
      <p>
        Tesbox needs a current browser with ES modules and Web Audio AudioWorklet: a Tesla with
        the MCU2 (Intel) or MCU3 (AMD) touchscreen computer on current software. The original
        MCU1 browser is not supported.
      </p>
      <p>Not affiliated with Tesla, Inc.</p>
    </div>
    <main id="app"></main>
    <script type="module" src="/src/main.ts"></script>
  </body>
</html>
```

The Unsupported block is visible by default and hidden by the module bundle, so a browser that
cannot run ES modules (MCU1) keeps showing it without any inline script (CSP-compatible).
`<meta name="color-scheme" content="dark">` gives dark default colours without CSS; the
`data:,` icon (allowed by `img-src 'self' data:`) stops a `/favicon.ico` request.

- [ ] **Step 21: Create `src/main.ts`**

```ts
import { BUILD_SHA } from './platform/build-info';

/** Phase 0 placeholder boot. The real app shell arrives in Phase 3. */
function boot(doc: Document): void {
  const unsupported = doc.getElementById('unsupported');
  if (unsupported) unsupported.hidden = true;

  const app = doc.getElementById('app');
  if (!app) return;

  const status = doc.createElement('p');
  status.id = 'status';
  const link = doc.createElement('a');
  link.href = '/probe/';
  link.textContent = '/probe/';
  status.append('Tesbox — under construction. Open ', link);

  const build = doc.createElement('p');
  build.id = 'build-sha';
  build.textContent = `Build ${BUILD_SHA}`;

  const footer = doc.createElement('p');
  footer.textContent = 'Not affiliated with Tesla, Inc.';

  app.replaceChildren(status, build, footer);
}

boot(document);
```

### Task 2: Platform — safe storage (`storage.ts`) and feature/MCU/viewport detection (`detect.ts`)

**Files:**
- Create: `src/platform/storage.ts`
- Create: `src/platform/detect.ts`
- Test: `tests/unit/storage.test.ts`
- Test: `tests/unit/detect.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1 tooling only (Vitest config, `tsconfig.json`). `minTouchCssPx` stays in `src/contracts.ts` and is not re-implemented or re-exported here.
- Produces:
  - `src/platform/storage.ts` (key prefix `"tesbox.v1."` added internally):
    - `export function storageAvailable(): boolean` — true only if a write + remove round-trip succeeds now.
    - `export function getJSON<T>(key: string, fallback: T): T` — missing key, unparseable JSON, a throwing getter/`getItem`, or a value whose `typeof`/array-ness differs from a non-null `fallback` (or `null` where the fallback is an object) → `fallback`. With `fallback === null` any parsed JSON is returned.
    - `export function setJSON(key: string, value: unknown): boolean` — false when storage is missing/throws/full or `value` is not JSON-serialisable (`undefined`, cycles, BigInt).
    - `export function remove(key: string): void` — never throws.
  - `src/platform/detect.ts`:
    - `export type Mcu = 'intel' | 'amd' | 'unknown'`
    - `export function parseMcu(renderer: string | null): Mcu` — Intel regex tested first: `/intel|mesa dri intel|hd graphics|apollolake|apl/i` → `'intel'`; then `/amd|radeon|raven|vega|picasso|ryzen/i` → `'amd'`; null/empty/other → `'unknown'`.
    - `export function getWebglRenderer(): string | null` — `WEBGL_debug_renderer_info` → `getParameter(UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL)`; null without `document`, WebGL, the extension, or a non-empty string; always calls `WEBGL_lose_context.loseContext()` on the throwaway context.
    - `export interface Features { audioWorklet; serviceWorker; cacheStorage; mediaSession; gamepad; deviceMotion; geolocation; crossOriginIsolated; fullscreen: boolean; esModules: true; webgl; outputTimestamp; mediaStreamDestination: boolean }` (exactly the contract fields) and `export function detectFeatures(): Features` — never throws: a `caches` getter that throws `SecurityError` (sandboxed, opaque-origin or storage-blocked contexts) yields `cacheStorage: false`.
    - `export interface ViewportInfo { dpr: number; screen: [number, number]; inner: [number, number]; visual: [number, number] | null; maxTouchPoints: number }` and `export function viewportInfo(): ViewportInfo` — Node/no-window → `{ dpr: 1, screen: [0, 0], inner: [0, 0], visual: null, maxTouchPoints: 0 }`.

**Cross-task premises:**
- Task 1 is done: `vite.config.ts` runs `tests/unit/**/*.test.ts` in the `node` environment, and `npm run lint` / `npm run typecheck` exist.
- T5 (`GpsRunner` crash marker under `CRASH_KEY = 'probe.gpsRun'`) and T9 (`'probe.visits'`, `'probe.firstSeen'`, `'probe.lastSeen'`) pass **un-prefixed** keys; this module adds `tesbox.v1.`. They should read objects/nullable strings with `getJSON(key, null)` and numbers with `getJSON(key, 0)`; a stored value of a different type yields the fallback.
- T4 (`summary.ts`) imports the types `Features`, `Mcu`, `ViewportInfo` from `src/platform/detect.ts`; T10 calls `detectFeatures()`, `viewportInfo()`, `getWebglRenderer()` and `parseMcu()`. T10 calls `detectFeatures()` at module top level, before its error handlers exist, so an exception there would stop the whole probe page from booting (Review Focus RF5; T12's `shell.spec.ts` loads the page with throwing `localStorage` and `caches` getters).

```ts
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { getJSON, remove, setJSON, storageAvailable } from '../../src/platform/storage';

/** Minimal in-memory Storage; flags make individual methods throw like a real browser can. */
class FakeStorage {
  readonly map = new Map<string, string>();
  throwOnSet = false;
  throwOnGet = false;
  throwOnRemove = false;
  get length(): number {
    return this.map.size;
  }
  key(index: number): string | null {
    return [...this.map.keys()][index] ?? null;
  }
  getItem(key: string): string | null {
    if (this.throwOnGet) throw new DOMException('denied', 'SecurityError');
    return this.map.get(key) ?? null;
  }
  setItem(key: string, value: string): void {
    if (this.throwOnSet) throw new DOMException('full', 'QuotaExceededError');
    this.map.set(key, String(value));
  }
  removeItem(key: string): void {
    if (this.throwOnRemove) throw new DOMException('denied', 'SecurityError');
    this.map.delete(key);
  }
  clear(): void {
    this.map.clear();
  }
}

const original = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(globalThis, 'localStorage');

function installValue(value: unknown): void {
  Object.defineProperty(globalThis, 'localStorage', { configurable: true, writable: true, value });
}

function installThrowingGetter(): void {
  Object.defineProperty(globalThis, 'localStorage', {
    configurable: true,
    get() {
      throw new DOMException('The operation is insecure.', 'SecurityError');
    },
  });
}

afterEach(() => {
  if (original) Object.defineProperty(globalThis, 'localStorage', original);
  else Reflect.deleteProperty(globalThis, 'localStorage');
});

describe('storage with a working localStorage', () => {
  let fake: FakeStorage;
  beforeEach(() => {
    fake = new FakeStorage();
    installValue(fake);
  });

  it('reports available and leaves no probe key behind', () => {
    expect(storageAvailable()).toBe(true);
    expect(fake.length).toBe(0);
  });

  it('round-trips JSON under the tesbox.v1. prefix', () => {
    expect(setJSON('probe.visits', 3)).toBe(true);
    expect(fake.map.get('tesbox.v1.probe.visits')).toBe('3');
    expect(getJSON('probe.visits', 0)).toBe(3);
    expect(setJSON('obj', { a: [1, 2], b: 'x' })).toBe(true);
    expect(getJSON('obj', { a: [] as number[], b: '' })).toEqual({ a: [1, 2], b: 'x' });
  });

  it('returns the fallback for a missing key', () => {
    expect(getJSON('missing', 'fb')).toBe('fb');
  });

  it('returns the fallback for corrupt JSON', () => {
    fake.map.set('tesbox.v1.bad', '{not json');
    expect(getJSON('bad', 7)).toBe(7);
  });

  it('returns the fallback when the stored type does not match the fallback type', () => {
    fake.map.set('tesbox.v1.n', '"seven"');
    fake.map.set('tesbox.v1.arr', '{"a":1}');
    fake.map.set('tesbox.v1.obj', 'null');
    expect(getJSON('n', 7)).toBe(7);
    expect(getJSON<number[]>('arr', [])).toEqual([]);
    expect(getJSON('obj', { a: 1 })).toEqual({ a: 1 });
  });

  it('returns any stored JSON when the fallback is null', () => {
    setJSON('crash', { mode: 'poll-hiacc-off', startedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z' });
    expect(getJSON<{ mode: string; startedAt: string } | null>('crash', null)).toEqual({
      mode: 'poll-hiacc-off',
      startedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
    });
  });

  it('refuses values JSON cannot represent', () => {
    const cyclic: Record<string, unknown> = {};
    cyclic.self = cyclic;
    expect(setJSON('cyclic', cyclic)).toBe(false);
    expect(setJSON('undef', undefined)).toBe(false);
    expect(setJSON('big', 10n)).toBe(false);
    expect(fake.length).toBe(0);
  });

  it('removes a key', () => {
    setJSON('k', 1);
    remove('k');
    expect(fake.map.has('tesbox.v1.k')).toBe(false);
    expect(getJSON('k', 0)).toBe(0);
  });

  it('keeps working across calls after a transient quota failure', () => {
    fake.throwOnSet = true;
    expect(setJSON('k', 1)).toBe(false);
    expect(storageAvailable()).toBe(false);
    fake.throwOnSet = false;
    expect(setJSON('k', 2)).toBe(true);
    expect(getJSON('k', 0)).toBe(2);
    expect(storageAvailable()).toBe(true);
  });
});

describe('RF5: storage that throws never throws to the caller', () => {
  it('handles a localStorage getter that throws', () => {
    installThrowingGetter();
    expect(() => storageAvailable()).not.toThrow();
    expect(storageAvailable()).toBe(false);
    expect(getJSON('k', 'fallback')).toBe('fallback');
    expect(setJSON('k', 1)).toBe(false);
    expect(() => remove('k')).not.toThrow();
  });

  it('handles setItem (and removeItem) that throw', () => {
    const fake = new FakeStorage();
    fake.throwOnSet = true;
    fake.throwOnRemove = true;
    installValue(fake);
    expect(storageAvailable()).toBe(false);
    expect(getJSON('k', { ok: true })).toEqual({ ok: true });
    expect(setJSON('k', 1)).toBe(false);
    expect(() => remove('k')).not.toThrow();
  });

  it('handles getItem that throws', () => {
    const fake = new FakeStorage();
    fake.throwOnGet = true;
    installValue(fake);
    expect(getJSON('k', 5)).toBe(5);
  });

  it('handles localStorage being absent', () => {
    installValue(undefined);
    expect(storageAvailable()).toBe(false);
    expect(getJSON('k', 1)).toBe(1);
    expect(setJSON('k', 1)).toBe(false);
    expect(() => remove('k')).not.toThrow();
  });
});
```

The tests install fakes with `Object.defineProperty(globalThis, 'localStorage', …)` and restore
the original descriptor afterwards, so they work whether or not the Node version exposes its own
experimental `localStorage`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `src/platform/storage.ts`**

```ts
/**
 * Safe localStorage wrapper (spec §10.3). Every key is namespaced "tesbox.v1." and every access
 * is wrapped in try/catch: the `localStorage` getter itself throws when site data is blocked,
 * and setItem throws when the quota is exhausted. Nothing here ever throws.
 */
const PREFIX = 'tesbox.v1.';
const PROBE_KEY = `${PREFIX}__probe__`;

function store(): Storage | null {
  try {
    return globalThis.localStorage ?? null;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

/** True when a write + remove round-trip succeeds right now. */
export function storageAvailable(): boolean {
  const s = store();
  if (!s) return false;
  try {
    s.setItem(PROBE_KEY, '1');
    s.removeItem(PROBE_KEY);
    return true;
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

/**
 * Reads and parses `tesbox.v1.<key>`. Missing, unparseable or type-mismatched values (a value
 * whose typeof / array-ness differs from a non-null `fallback`) return `fallback`.
 */
export function getJSON<T>(key: string, fallback: T): T {
  const s = store();
  if (!s) return fallback;
  try {
    const raw = s.getItem(PREFIX + key);
    if (raw === null) return fallback;
    const parsed: unknown = JSON.parse(raw);
    if (fallback !== null && fallback !== undefined) {
      if (typeof parsed !== typeof fallback) return fallback;
      if (Array.isArray(parsed) !== Array.isArray(fallback)) return fallback;
      if (typeof fallback === 'object' && parsed === null) return fallback;
    }
    return parsed as T;
  } catch {
    return fallback;
  }
}

/** Stores JSON under `tesbox.v1.<key>`. False when storage is unavailable, full, or the value is not serialisable. */
export function setJSON(key: string, value: unknown): boolean {
  const s = store();
  if (!s) return false;
  try {
    const json = JSON.stringify(value);
    if (typeof json !== 'string') return false;
    s.setItem(PREFIX + key, json);
    return true;
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

/** Removes `tesbox.v1.<key>`; silently does nothing when storage is unavailable. */
export function remove(key: string): void {
  try {
    store()?.removeItem(PREFIX + key);
  } catch {
    // storage unavailable: nothing to remove
  }
}
```

`store()` reads `globalThis.localStorage` inside `try` because the getter itself throws a
`SecurityError` when site data is blocked; every Storage call is wrapped again because `setItem`
throws `QuotaExceededError`.

```ts
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';

  detectFeatures,
  getWebglRenderer,

interface FakeGlOptions {
  renderer?: unknown;
  debugExt?: boolean;
}

/** Fake `document` whose canvases hand out a fake WebGL context; records loseContext calls. */
function stubDocument(gl: FakeGlOptions | null, extra: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
  const lost = { count: 0 };
  const UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL = 0x9246;
  const context =
    gl === null
      ? null
      : {
          getExtension(name: string): unknown {
            if (name === 'WEBGL_debug_renderer_info') {
              return gl.debugExt === false ? null : { UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL };
            }
            if (name === 'WEBGL_lose_context') {
              return {
                loseContext() {
                  lost.count += 1;
                },
              };
            }
            return null;
          },
          getParameter(p: number): unknown {
            return p === UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL ? gl.renderer : null;
          },
        };
  vi.stubGlobal('document', {
    createElement: (tag: string) => {
      if (tag !== 'canvas') throw new Error(`unexpected element ${tag}`);
      return { getContext: (id: string) => (id === 'webgl' ? context : null) };
    },
    ...extra,
  });
  return lost;
}

      getGamepads: () => [],
    });
    stubDocument({ renderer: 'x' }, { fullscreenEnabled: true });
    expect(detectFeatures()).toEqual({
      audioWorklet: true,
      serviceWorker: true,
      cacheStorage: true,
      mediaSession: true,
      gamepad: true,
      deviceMotion: true,
      geolocation: true,
      crossOriginIsolated: true,
      fullscreen: true,
      esModules: true,
      webgl: true,
      outputTimestamp: true,
      mediaStreamDestination: true,
    });
  });

  it('needs both AudioWorkletNode and context.audioWorklet for audioWorklet', () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('AudioContext', class {});
    vi.stubGlobal('AudioWorkletNode', class {});
    // The bare class also lacks getOutputTimestamp and createMediaStreamDestination.
    expect(detectFeatures()).toMatchObject({ audioWorklet: false, outputTimestamp: false, mediaStreamDestination: false });
  });

  it('survives a caches getter that throws (sandboxed / storage-blocked context)', () => {
    Object.defineProperty(globalThis, 'caches', {
      configurable: true,
      get() {
        throw new DOMException('denied', 'SecurityError');
      },
    });
    try {
      expect(() => detectFeatures()).not.toThrow();
      expect(detectFeatures().cacheStorage).toBe(false);
    } finally {
      Reflect.deleteProperty(globalThis, 'caches');
    }
  });
});

describe('viewportInfo', () => {
  it('returns neutral values without a window (Node)', () => {
    expect(viewportInfo()).toEqual({
      dpr: 1,
      screen: [0, 0],
      inner: [0, 0],
      visual: null,
      maxTouchPoints: 0,
    });
  });

  it('reads dpr, screen, inner and visual viewport sizes', () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('window', {
      devicePixelRatio: 1.53,
      screen: { width: 1182, height: 920 },
      innerWidth: 773,
      innerHeight: 601,
      visualViewport: { width: 773, height: 590.5 },
    });
    vi.stubGlobal('navigator', { maxTouchPoints: 10 });
    expect(viewportInfo()).toEqual({
      dpr: 1.53,
      screen: [1182, 920],
      inner: [773, 601],
      visual: [773, 590.5],
      maxTouchPoints: 10,
    });
  });

  it('defaults dpr to 1, visual to null and touch points to 0 when missing', () => {
    vi.stubGlobal('window', {
      devicePixelRatio: 0,
      screen: { width: 800, height: 600 },
      innerWidth: 800,
      innerHeight: 600,
      visualViewport: null,
    });
    vi.stubGlobal('navigator', {});
    expect(viewportInfo()).toEqual({
      dpr: 1,
      screen: [800, 600],
      inner: [800, 600],
      visual: null,
      maxTouchPoints: 0,
    });
  });
});
```

Node 22+ defines a global `navigator`; tests that need a bare or browser-like navigator replace it
with `vi.stubGlobal`, and `vi.unstubAllGlobals()` restores everything after each test. The
renderer strings are representative Mesa/ANGLE formats, not captured from a specific car.

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement `src/platform/detect.ts`**

```ts
/**
 * Feature detection, MCU guess and viewport facts (spec §3). Every probe is guarded so this
 * module also loads in Node (unit tests) and in browsers that lack an API.
 * Touch-target sizing lives in src/contracts.ts (`minTouchCssPx`), not here.
 */

export type Mcu = 'intel' | 'amd' | 'unknown';

const INTEL_RE = /intel|mesa dri intel|hd graphics|apollolake|apl/i;
const AMD_RE = /amd|radeon|raven|vega|picasso|ryzen/i;

/** Guesses the Tesla MCU from a WebGL renderer string: Intel Atom (MCU2) or AMD Ryzen (MCU3). */
export function parseMcu(renderer: string | null): Mcu {
  if (!renderer) return 'unknown';
  if (INTEL_RE.test(renderer)) return 'intel';
  if (AMD_RE.test(renderer)) return 'amd';
  return 'unknown';
}

/** Creates a throwaway WebGL context, runs `fn`, then releases the context (GPU memory is scarce on MCU2). */
function withWebgl<T>(fn: (gl: WebGLRenderingContext) => T, fallback: T): T {
  if (typeof document === 'undefined') return fallback;
  try {
    const canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
    const gl = canvas.getContext('webgl');
    if (!gl) return fallback;
    try {
      return fn(gl);
    } finally {
      gl.getExtension('WEBGL_lose_context')?.loseContext();
    }
  } catch {
    return fallback;
  }
}

/** Unmasked renderer via WEBGL_debug_renderer_info; null when WebGL or the extension is unavailable. */
export function getWebglRenderer(): string | null {
  return withWebgl<string | null>((gl) => {
    const ext = gl.getExtension('WEBGL_debug_renderer_info');
    if (!ext) return null;
    const renderer: unknown = gl.getParameter(ext.UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL);
    return typeof renderer === 'string' && renderer !== '' ? renderer : null;
  }, null);
}

export interface Features {
  audioWorklet: boolean;
  serviceWorker: boolean;
  cacheStorage: boolean;
  mediaSession: boolean;
  gamepad: boolean;
  deviceMotion: boolean;
  geolocation: boolean;
  crossOriginIsolated: boolean;
  fullscreen: boolean;
  esModules: true;
  webgl: boolean;
  outputTimestamp: boolean;
  mediaStreamDestination: boolean;
}

/** True when AudioContext exists and its prototype chain has `name` (BaseAudioContext members included). */
function audioContextHas(name: string): boolean {
  return typeof AudioContext === 'function' && name in AudioContext.prototype;
}

/** `caches` is a getter that throws SecurityError in sandboxed or storage-blocked contexts. */
function hasCaches(): boolean {
  try {
    return typeof caches !== 'undefined';
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

/** Never throws: every probe is guarded, so the probe page can call this at module top level. */
export function detectFeatures(): Features {
  const nav: Navigator | undefined = typeof navigator === 'undefined' ? undefined : navigator;
  return {
    audioWorklet: typeof AudioWorkletNode === 'function' && audioContextHas('audioWorklet'),
    serviceWorker: nav !== undefined && 'serviceWorker' in nav,
    cacheStorage: hasCaches(),
    mediaSession: nav !== undefined && 'mediaSession' in nav,
    gamepad: nav !== undefined && typeof nav.getGamepads === 'function',
    deviceMotion: typeof DeviceMotionEvent !== 'undefined',
    geolocation: nav !== undefined && 'geolocation' in nav,
    crossOriginIsolated: globalThis.crossOriginIsolated === true,
    fullscreen: typeof document !== 'undefined' && document.fullscreenEnabled === true,
    // This module only runs from an ES-module bundle, so module support is a given.
    esModules: true,
    webgl: withWebgl(() => true, false),
    outputTimestamp: audioContextHas('getOutputTimestamp'),
    mediaStreamDestination: audioContextHas('createMediaStreamDestination'),
  };
}

export interface ViewportInfo {
  dpr: number;
  screen: [number, number];
  inner: [number, number];
  visual: [number, number] | null;
  maxTouchPoints: number;
}

/** Current viewport facts in CSS px (re-read on resize; Tesla zoom changes dpr at runtime). */
export function viewportInfo(): ViewportInfo {
  if (typeof window === 'undefined') {
    return { dpr: 1, screen: [0, 0], inner: [0, 0], visual: null, maxTouchPoints: 0 };
  }
  const vv = window.visualViewport;
  return {
    dpr: window.devicePixelRatio || 1,
    screen: [window.screen.width, window.screen.height],
    inner: [window.innerWidth, window.innerHeight],
    visual: vv ? [vv.width, vv.height] : null,
    maxTouchPoints: typeof navigator === 'undefined' ? 0 : (navigator.maxTouchPoints ?? 0),
  };
}
```

`'audioWorklet' in AudioContext.prototype` walks the prototype chain to `BaseAudioContext`, where
Chromium defines it; it is absent in insecure contexts, matching where AudioWorklet really works.
Each WebGL probe creates one throwaway context and releases it immediately (MCU2 GPUs are tight).
`typeof caches` still runs the `window.caches` getter, which throws `SecurityError` in sandboxed,
opaque-origin or storage-blocked contexts, so `hasCaches()` wraps it in `try`.

### Task 3: In-house QR encoder `qr.ts` — ISO/IEC 18004 byte mode, ECC L, versions 1–40, masks + penalties, chunking, canvas drawing

**Files:**
- Create: `src/platform/qr.ts`
- Test: `tests/unit/qr.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `jsqr` (devDependency installed by Task 1; tests only): `import jsQR from 'jsqr'`, `jsQR(data: Uint8ClampedArray, width: number, height: number, options?: { inversionAttempts?: 'dontInvert' | 'onlyInvert' | 'attemptBoth' | 'invertFirst' }): QRCode | null` with `QRCode.data: string`, `QRCode.binaryData: number[]`, `QRCode.version: number`.
- Produces (contract API, used by application code):
  - `export interface QrMatrix { size: number; modules: Uint8Array }` — `size = 17 + 4·version`, row-major, `1` = dark.
  - `export function encodeQr(text: string): QrMatrix` — UTF-8 bytes, byte mode, ECC L, smallest fitting version (version 23 is skipped → 24, see premises), lowest-penalty mask (ties → lowest mask number). Throws `RangeError` when the UTF-8 length exceeds 2953 bytes.
  - `export function chunkText(text: string, maxBytes = 1100): string[]` — payload slices of ≤ `maxBytes` UTF-8 bytes, never split inside a code point, each prefixed `TBX i/n\n` (1-based `i`); `maxBytes` bounds the payload, not the header. Empty text → `['TBX 1/1\n']`. Throws `RangeError` if `maxBytes` is not an integer ≥ 4.
  - `export function drawQr(canvas: HTMLCanvasElement, m: QrMatrix, scale = 6, quiet = 4): void` — sets `canvas.width = canvas.height = (m.size + 2·quiet)·scale`, paints white background then black modules; throws `Error('2D canvas context unavailable')` if `getContext('2d')` is null.
- Produces (extra exports for tests only; application code must not depend on them):
  - `export function qrByteCapacityL(version: number): number` (RangeError outside integer 1–40)
  - `export function reedSolomonEcc(data: Uint8Array, eccLen: number): Uint8Array`
  - `export function qrPenalty(m: QrMatrix): number` (N1 + N2 + N3 + N4)
  - `export function encodeQrWithMask(text: string, mask: number): QrMatrix` (RangeError for mask outside integer 0–7)

**Cross-task premises:**
- Task 1 is done and `jsqr` ^1.4.0 is in `devDependencies` (`ls node_modules/jsqr/dist/index.d.ts` exists).
- T10 export flow: `chunkText(JSON.stringify(summary))` → for each chunk `drawQr(canvas, encodeQr(chunk))` with a “QR i/n” caption; with the default `maxBytes` every chunk is ≤ 1100 + 14 header bytes, far below 2953, so `encodeQr` never throws there. CSS sizing of the canvas is T10's.
- T12's `probe-qr.spec.ts` decodes with `jsqr` 1.4.0 and reassembles by dropping each chunk's first line and concatenating in order. jsQR 1.4.0 has wrong version-23 alignment centres (`6, 30, 54, 74, 102`; ISO says `6, 30, 54, 78, 102`), so a v23 symbol from a correct encoder is unreadable by it; this encoder never emits version 23 (payloads of 1004–1091 bytes use version 24), so every chunk T10 renders is jsQR-readable.
- No other task imports `qrByteCapacityL`, `reedSolomonEcc`, `qrPenalty` or `encodeQrWithMask`.
- Provenance (spec §2): the layout, BCH, Reed–Solomon, codeword-placement and mask-selection code is adapted from Project Nayuki's MIT-licensed *QR Code generator library* (TypeScript). The attribution in `qr.ts`'s header comment must stay, and it is a legal comment (`/*!` … `@license`): T1's `vite.config.ts` sets `build.rolldownOptions.output.comments: { legal: true }` (Vite 8.3 strips legal comments when minifying otherwise — checked: without it `dist/assets/probe-*.js` has no `Nayuki`), so the attribution stays inside the minified `dist/assets/probe-*.js` that every browser loads. T12 ships the full MIT notice in `NOTICES`, copies `NOTICES` and `LICENSE` into the Docker image's web root (`/NOTICES.txt`, `/LICENSE.txt`; asserted by `repo-files.test.ts`) and logs the source in `docs/CLEAN_ROOM.md`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the first failing tests (capacity table + Reed–Solomon): create `tests/unit/qr.test.ts`**

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { qrByteCapacityL, reedSolomonEcc } from '../../src/platform/qr';

// Byte-mode capacities at level L, versions 1–40 (ISO/IEC 18004 Table 7).
const CAPACITY_L = [
  17, 32, 53, 78, 106, 134, 154, 192, 230, 271, 321, 367, 425, 458, 520, 586, 644, 718, 792, 858,
  929, 1003, 1091, 1171, 1273, 1367, 1465, 1528, 1628, 1732, 1840, 1952, 2068, 2188, 2303, 2431,
  2563, 2699, 2809, 2953,
];

describe('qrByteCapacityL', () => {
  it('matches the ISO byte-mode capacity table for every version', () => {
    const computed = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => qrByteCapacityL(i + 1));
    expect(computed).toEqual(CAPACITY_L);
  });

  it('rejects versions outside 1–40', () => {
    expect(() => qrByteCapacityL(0)).toThrow(RangeError);
    expect(() => qrByteCapacityL(41)).toThrow(RangeError);
    expect(() => qrByteCapacityL(1.5)).toThrow(RangeError);
  });
});

// Independent GF(256) arithmetic for the syndrome check (does not reuse the module's tables).
function gfMulRef(a: number, b: number): number {
  let product = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < 8; i++) {
    if (b & 1) product ^= a;
    b >>= 1;
    a <<= 1;
    if (a & 0x100) a ^= 0x11d;
  }
  return product;
}

describe('reedSolomonEcc', () => {
  it('reproduces the published 1-M "HELLO WORLD" example', () => {
    const data = Uint8Array.of(
      32, 91, 11, 120, 209, 114, 220, 77, 67, 64, 236, 17, 236, 17, 236, 17,
    );
    expect(Array.from(reedSolomonEcc(data, 10))).toEqual([
      196, 35, 39, 119, 235, 215, 231, 226, 93, 23,
    ]);
  });

  it.each([7, 10, 18, 22, 26, 30])(
    'yields a codeword with zero syndromes at α^0..α^%i−1',
    (eccLen) => {
      const data = Uint8Array.from({ length: 50 }, (_, i) => (i * 37 + eccLen) & 0xff);
      const codeword = [...data, ...reedSolomonEcc(data, eccLen)];
      let alphaI = 1;
      for (let i = 0; i < eccLen; i++) {
        let acc = 0; // Horner evaluation, highest power first
        for (const c of codeword) acc = gfMulRef(acc, alphaI) ^ c;
        expect(acc).toBe(0);
        alphaI = gfMulRef(alphaI, 2);
      }
    },
  );
});
```

`CAPACITY_L` is ISO/IEC 18004 Table 7 (byte mode, level L). The Reed–Solomon vector is the widely
published 1-M “HELLO WORLD” worked example; the syndrome test checks the defining RS property
(codeword evaluates to 0 at α⁰…α^(n−1)) with an independent shift-and-add GF(256) multiply.

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `src/platform/qr.ts` with the tables, capacity and Reed–Solomon code**

```ts
/*!
 * In-house QR Code encoder after ISO/IEC 18004: byte mode, error-correction level L,
 * versions 1–40, all eight data masks scored with penalty rules N1–N4. Layout, BCH,
 * Reed–Solomon, codeword-placement and mask-selection code adapted from Project Nayuki's
 * "QR Code generator library" (TypeScript), Copyright (c) Project Nayuki, MIT License,
 * https://www.nayuki.io/page/qr-code-generator-library (full notice in NOTICES). Used only
 * for the statistics-only diagnostics export, never on an audio path, so clarity beats speed
 * here.
 * @license MIT — Copyright (c) Project Nayuki. (MIT License)
 */

export interface QrMatrix {
  /** Modules per side: 17 + 4 × version. */
  size: number;
  /** size × size, row-major, 1 = dark. */
  modules: Uint8Array;
}

/** EC codewords per block, level L, versions 1–40 (ISO/IEC 18004 Table 9). */
const ECC_PER_BLOCK_L = [
  7, 10, 15, 20, 26, 18, 20, 24, 30, 18, 20, 24, 26, 30, 22, 24, 28, 30, 28, 28, 28, 28, 30, 30,
  26, 28, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30, 30,
];

/** Number of EC blocks, level L, versions 1–40 (ISO/IEC 18004 Table 9). */
const ECC_BLOCKS_L = [
  1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 2, 4, 4, 4, 4, 4, 6, 6, 6, 6, 7, 8, 8, 9, 9, 10, 12, 12, 12, 13, 14,
  15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 19, 20, 21, 22, 24, 25,
];

function tableAt(table: readonly number[], version: number): number {
  const v = table[version - 1];
  if (v === undefined) throw new RangeError(`QR version out of range: ${version}`);
  return v;
}

/**
 * Modules left for data + EC after all function patterns, derived from the symbol layout:
 * size² − 3 finders with separators (3 × 64) − format info and dark module (31) − timing
 * (2 × (size − 16)) = (16v + 128)v + 64; minus alignment patterns 25(n² − 3) less the
 * 10(n − 2) modules they share with the timing lines, n = ⌊v/7⌋ + 2 (v ≥ 2); minus version
 * info 2 × 18 (v ≥ 7).
 */
function rawDataModules(version: number): number {
  let result = (16 * version + 128) * version + 64;
  if (version >= 2) {
    const n = Math.floor(version / 7) + 2;
    result -= (25 * n - 10) * n - 55;
    if (version >= 7) result -= 36;
  }
  return result;
}

function totalCodewords(version: number): number {
  return Math.floor(rawDataModules(version) / 8);
}

function dataCodewordsL(version: number): number {
  return (
    totalCodewords(version) - tableAt(ECC_PER_BLOCK_L, version) * tableAt(ECC_BLOCKS_L, version)
  );
}

function countBits(version: number): number {
  return version <= 9 ? 8 : 16;
}

/** Largest UTF-8 byte payload that fits a version-`version` level-L symbol in byte mode. */
export function qrByteCapacityL(version: number): number {
  if (!Number.isInteger(version) || version < 1 || version > 40) {
    throw new RangeError(`QR version out of range: ${version}`);
  }
  return Math.floor((dataCodewordsL(version) * 8 - 4 - countBits(version)) / 8);
}

// ───────────────────────────── GF(256) and Reed–Solomon ─────────────────────────────

/** GF(2⁸) with the QR primitive polynomial x⁸ + x⁴ + x³ + x² + 1 (0x11D), generator α = 2. */
const GF_EXP = new Uint8Array(512);
const GF_LOG = new Uint8Array(256);
{
  let x = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i < 255; i++) {
    GF_EXP[i] = x;
    GF_EXP[i + 255] = x;
    GF_LOG[x] = i;
    x <<= 1;
    if (x & 0x100) x ^= 0x11d;
  }
}

function gfMul(a: number, b: number): number {
  if (a === 0 || b === 0) return 0;
  return GF_EXP[(GF_LOG[a] ?? 0) + (GF_LOG[b] ?? 0)] ?? 0;
}

const generatorCache = new Map<number, Uint8Array>();

/** g(x) = Π_{i=0}^{degree−1} (x − αⁱ), coefficients highest power first (leading 1 included). */
function generatorPoly(degree: number): Uint8Array {
  const cached = generatorCache.get(degree);
  if (cached) return cached;
  let poly = Uint8Array.of(1);
  for (let i = 0; i < degree; i++) {
    const root = GF_EXP[i] ?? 0;
    const next = new Uint8Array(poly.length + 1);
    for (let j = 0; j < poly.length; j++) {
      const c = poly[j] ?? 0;
      next[j] = (next[j] ?? 0) ^ c;
      next[j + 1] = (next[j + 1] ?? 0) ^ gfMul(c, root);
    }
    poly = next;
  }
  generatorCache.set(degree, poly);
  return poly;
}

/** Reed–Solomon EC codewords: remainder of data(x) · x^eccLen divided by g(x). */
export function reedSolomonEcc(data: Uint8Array, eccLen: number): Uint8Array {
  const gen = generatorPoly(eccLen);
  const rem = new Uint8Array(eccLen);
  for (const byte of data) {
    const factor = byte ^ (rem[0] ?? 0);
    rem.copyWithin(0, 1);
    rem[eccLen - 1] = 0;
    for (let j = 0; j < eccLen; j++) rem[j] = (rem[j] ?? 0) ^ gfMul(gen[j + 1] ?? 0, factor);
  }
  return rem;
}
```

`rawDataModules` is derived in its comment from the symbol layout (finders + separators, format
info, timing, alignment patterns less their timing overlap, version info); dividing by 8 gives
ISO's total codewords (26 for version 1 … 3706 for version 40). Index reads use `?? 0` because of
`noUncheckedIndexedAccess`. The header opens with `/*!` and carries `@license`, so the minifier
keeps it as a legal comment (with T1's `comments: { legal: true }`); keep both markers.

- [ ] **Step 5: Extend the test header for symbol tests: replace the first two lines of `tests/unit/qr.test.ts`**

Replace exactly these two lines:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { qrByteCapacityL, reedSolomonEcc } from '../../src/platform/qr';
```

with this block (imports plus helpers shared by the remaining tests):

```ts
import jsQR from 'jsqr';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
  encodeQr,
  encodeQrWithMask,
  qrByteCapacityL,
  qrPenalty,
  reedSolomonEcc,
  type QrMatrix,
} from '../../src/platform/qr';

/** Renders a matrix to RGBA pixels (black on white, light quiet zone) for jsQR. */
function toImage(m: QrMatrix, scale = 3, quiet = 4) {
  const width = (m.size + quiet * 2) * scale;
  const data = new Uint8ClampedArray(width * width * 4).fill(255);
  for (let y = 0; y < m.size; y++) {
    for (let x = 0; x < m.size; x++) {
      if (m.modules[y * m.size + x] !== 1) continue;
      for (let sy = 0; sy < scale; sy++) {
        for (let sx = 0; sx < scale; sx++) {
          const p = (((y + quiet) * scale + sy) * width + (x + quiet) * scale + sx) * 4;
          data[p] = 0;
          data[p + 1] = 0;
          data[p + 2] = 0;
        }
      }
    }
  }
  return { data, width };
}

function decode(m: QrMatrix) {
  const { data, width } = toImage(m);
  const result = jsQR(data, width, width, { inversionAttempts: 'dontInvert' });
  if (!result) throw new Error(`jsQR could not decode a ${m.size}×${m.size} symbol`);
  return result;
}

const utf8 = (s: string): number[] => Array.from(new TextEncoder().encode(s));

/** Deterministic ASCII filler of exactly n bytes. */
function ascii(n: number, seed = 0): string {
  let out = '';
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out += String.fromCharCode(33 + ((i * 7 + seed * 13) % 94));
  return out;
}

const versionOf = (m: QrMatrix): number => (m.size - 17) / 4;
```

```ts
describe('encodeQr', () => {
  it('encodes a short string as version 1 and decodes back', () => {
    const m = encodeQr('TBX 1/1\n{"v":1}');
    expect(m.size).toBe(21);
    expect(m.modules.length).toBe(21 * 21);
    expect(m.modules.every((v) => v === 0 || v === 1)).toBe(true);
    expect(decode(m).data).toBe('TBX 1/1\n{"v":1}');
  });

  it('encodes UTF-8 text as bytes', () => {
    const text = 'Tesbox — 2.5 Turbo Inline-5 ✓ 🚗';
    const result = decode(encodeQr(text));
    expect(result.binaryData).toEqual(utf8(text));
    expect(result.data).toBe(text);
  });

  it('encodes the empty string', () => {
    const result = decode(encodeQr(''));
    expect(result.binaryData).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('picks the smallest version and switches exactly at capacity', () => {
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(17)))).toBe(1);
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(18)))).toBe(2);
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(230)))).toBe(9);
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(231)))).toBe(10);
  });

  it('round-trips a full-capacity payload at every version 1–40 through jsQR', () => {
    for (let version = 1; version <= 40; version++) {
      const text = ascii(qrByteCapacityL(version), version);
      const m = encodeQr(text);
      const expected = version === 23 ? 24 : version; // version 23 is never emitted
      expect(versionOf(m)).toBe(expected);
      const result = decode(m);
      expect(result.version).toBe(expected);
      expect(result.data).toBe(text);
    }
  }, 60_000);

  it('skips version 23: payloads in its range use version 24', () => {
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(1003)))).toBe(22);
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(1004)))).toBe(24);
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(1091)))).toBe(24);
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(1092)))).toBe(24);
  });

  // Alignment centres per ISO/IEC 18004 Annex E; each must hold a 5×5 dark/light/dark pattern.
  it.each([
    [2, [6, 18]],
    [7, [6, 22, 38]],
    [22, [6, 26, 50, 74, 98]],
    [24, [6, 28, 54, 80, 106]],
    [32, [6, 34, 60, 86, 112, 138]],
    [40, [6, 30, 58, 86, 114, 142, 170]],
  ])('places version %i alignment patterns at %j', (version, centres) => {
    const m = encodeQr(ascii(qrByteCapacityL(version)));
    expect(versionOf(m)).toBe(version);
    const at = (x: number, y: number) => m.modules[y * m.size + x];
    const last = centres.length - 1;
    centres.forEach((cy, i) => {
      centres.forEach((cx, j) => {
        if ((i === 0 && j === 0) || (i === 0 && j === last) || (i === last && j === 0)) return;
        for (let dy = -2; dy <= 2; dy++) {
          for (let dx = -2; dx <= 2; dx++) {
            const ring = Math.max(Math.abs(dx), Math.abs(dy));
            expect(at(cx + dx, cy + dy)).toBe(ring === 1 ? 0 : 1);
          }
        }
      });
    });
  });

  it('accepts exactly 2953 bytes and throws RangeError above', () => {
    expect(versionOf(encodeQr(ascii(2953)))).toBe(40);
    expect(() => encodeQr(ascii(2954))).toThrow(RangeError);
    expect(() => encodeQr('é'.repeat(1477))).toThrow(RangeError); // 2954 UTF-8 bytes
  });

  it.each([0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7])('decodes with forced mask %i (version 1 and 7)', (mask) => {
    for (const text of [ascii(15, mask), ascii(150, mask)]) {
      expect(decode(encodeQrWithMask(text, mask)).data).toBe(text);
    }
  });

  it('rejects an out-of-range forced mask', () => {
    expect(() => encodeQrWithMask('x', 8)).toThrow(RangeError);
    expect(() => encodeQrWithMask('x', -1)).toThrow(RangeError);
  });

  it('chooses a mask with the minimum penalty', () => {
    for (const text of ['hello', ascii(100), ascii(400, 3)]) {
      const chosen = qrPenalty(encodeQr(text));
      for (let mask = 0; mask < 8; mask++) {
        expect(chosen).toBeLessThanOrEqual(qrPenalty(encodeQrWithMask(text, mask)));
      }
    }
  });

  it('writes the published format bits for level L, mask 0', () => {
    const m = encodeQrWithMask('x', 0);
    // Copy 1 bits 14..9 sit in row 8, columns 0..5; bit 8 at (7,8); bit 7 at (8,8).
    const row8 = (x: number) => m.modules[8 * m.size + x];
    const col8 = (y: number) => m.modules[y * m.size + 8];
    const bits = [
      row8(0), row8(1), row8(2), row8(3), row8(4), row8(5), row8(7), col8(8),
      col8(7), col8(5), col8(4), col8(3), col8(2), col8(1), col8(0),
    ].join('');
    expect(bits).toBe('111011111000100');
  });

  it('writes the published version-7 information block', () => {
    const m = encodeQr(ascii(150));
    expect(m.size).toBe(45);
    // Bottom-left block: bit i at column ⌊i/3⌋, row size−11+(i mod 3); bit 0 = LSB.
    let value = 0;
    for (let i = 17; i >= 0; i--) {
      const row = m.size - 11 + (i % 3);
      value = (value << 1) | (m.modules[row * m.size + Math.floor(i / 3)] ?? 0);
    }
    expect(value).toBe(0b000111110010010100);
  });
});

describe('qrPenalty', () => {
  const matrix = (rows: string[]): QrMatrix => ({
    size: rows.length,
    modules: Uint8Array.from(rows.join('').split(''), Number),
  });

  it('scores an all-light 5×5 symbol: N1 30 + N2 48 + N4 90', () => {
    expect(qrPenalty(matrix(['00000', '00000', '00000', '00000', '00000']))).toBe(168);
  });

  it('scores a 5×5 checkerboard as 0', () => {
    expect(qrPenalty(matrix(['10101', '01010', '10101', '01010', '10101']))).toBe(0);
  });

  // 11×11 checkerboard: no runs ≥ 5, no 2×2 blocks, 61/121 dark → penalty 0. Replacing row 0
  // keeps every row run ≤ 4, every column run ≤ 2, no 2×2 block and N4 = 0, so any change
  // comes from N3 alone.
  const board = Array.from({ length: 11 }, (_, i) =>
    i % 2 === 0 ? '10101010101' : '01010101010',
  );
  const withRow0 = (row0: string): QrMatrix => matrix([row0, ...board.slice(1)]);

  it('scores the 11×11 checkerboard as 0', () => {
    expect(qrPenalty(matrix(board))).toBe(0);
  });

  // 13×13 variant: wide enough to put a DARK module on the far side of the pattern, so the
  // "preceded OR followed by four light modules" rule is distinguishable from "AND".
  const board13 = Array.from({ length: 13 }, (_, i) => (i % 2 === 0 ? '1010101010101' : '0101010101010'));
  const withRow0of13 = (row0: string): QrMatrix => matrix([row0, ...board13.slice(1)]);

  it('adds N3 = 40 for 1:1:3:1:1 followed by four light modules (dark module before it)', () => {
    expect(qrPenalty(withRow0of13('1101110100001'))).toBe(40);
  });

  it('adds N3 = 40 for 1:1:3:1:1 preceded by four light modules (dark module after it)', () => {
    expect(qrPenalty(withRow0of13('1000010111011'))).toBe(40);
  });

  it('adds no N3 when neither side has four light modules', () => {
    expect(qrPenalty(withRow0('11011101111'))).toBe(0);
  });
});
```

What these pin down: version selection exactly at the capacity boundaries (including the 8→16-bit
count switch at version 10), a jsQR round trip at full capacity for **every** version 1–40, the
ISO alignment centres (Annex E, version 32's irregular 26-module step included), all eight mask
formulas, minimum-penalty mask choice, the published format string for L/mask 0
(`111011111000100`) and version-7 information (`000111110010010100`), and hand-computed N1–N4
scores (all-light 5×5: rows 5×3 + columns 5×3 = 30, sixteen 2×2 blocks × 3 = 48, 0 % dark → k = 9
→ 90).

- [ ] **Step 8: Append the symbol construction to the end of `src/platform/qr.ts`**

```ts
// ───────────────────────────── Symbol constants ─────────────────────────────

/** Byte-mode capacity of version 40-L. */
const MAX_BYTES = 2953;

/** Format-information EC level bits: L = 01. */
const ECL_BITS_L = 1;

/**
 * Never emitted: jsQR ≤ 1.4.0 (our e2e decoder, also inside some web scanner apps) has version
 * 23's alignment centres as 6/30/54/74/102 instead of ISO's 6/30/54/78/102 and cannot read it.
 * Payloads that would fit version 23 use version 24.
 */
const SKIPPED_VERSION = 23;

// ───────────────────────────── Data codewords ─────────────────────────────

function encodeDataCodewords(bytes: Uint8Array, version: number): Uint8Array {
  const capacity = dataCodewordsL(version);
  const out = new Uint8Array(capacity);
  let bitLen = 0;
  const put = (value: number, length: number): void => {
    for (let i = length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      if ((value >>> i) & 1) {
        const index = bitLen >>> 3;
        out[index] = (out[index] ?? 0) | (0x80 >>> (bitLen & 7));
      }
      bitLen++;
    }
  };
  put(0b0100, 4); // byte-mode indicator
  put(bytes.length, countBits(version));
  for (const b of bytes) put(b, 8);
  put(0, Math.min(4, capacity * 8 - bitLen)); // terminator
  let index = Math.ceil(bitLen / 8); // zero bits up to the byte boundary are already in `out`
  for (let pad = 0xec; index < capacity; index++, pad ^= 0xec ^ 0x11) out[index] = pad;
  return out;
}

/** Splits data into the version's blocks, appends each block's EC, interleaves (ISO 7.6). */
function interleaveWithEcc(data: Uint8Array, version: number): Uint8Array {
  const numBlocks = tableAt(ECC_BLOCKS_L, version);
  const eccLen = tableAt(ECC_PER_BLOCK_L, version);
  const total = totalCodewords(version);
  const numShort = numBlocks - (total % numBlocks);
  const shortDataLen = Math.floor(total / numBlocks) - eccLen;
  const blocks: Uint8Array[] = [];
  const eccs: Uint8Array[] = [];
  let offset = 0;
  for (let b = 0; b < numBlocks; b++) {
    const len = shortDataLen + (b < numShort ? 0 : 1);
    const block = data.subarray(offset, offset + len);
    offset += len;
    blocks.push(block);
    eccs.push(reedSolomonEcc(block, eccLen));
  }
  const out = new Uint8Array(total);
  let o = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i <= shortDataLen; i++) {
    for (const block of blocks) {
      const v = block[i];
      if (v !== undefined) out[o++] = v;
    }
  }
  for (let i = 0; i < eccLen; i++) {
    for (const ecc of eccs) out[o++] = ecc[i] ?? 0;
  }
  return out;
}

// ───────────────────────────── Matrix construction ─────────────────────────────

class Grid {
  readonly size: number;
  readonly modules: Uint8Array;
  /** 1 where a function pattern lives (never masked, never holds data). */
  readonly isFunction: Uint8Array;
  constructor(size: number) {
    this.size = size;
    this.modules = new Uint8Array(size * size);
    this.isFunction = new Uint8Array(size * size);
  }
  setFunction(x: number, y: number, dark: boolean): void {
    const i = y * this.size + x;
    this.modules[i] = dark ? 1 : 0;
    this.isFunction[i] = 1;
  }
}

function bit(value: number, i: number): boolean {
  return ((value >>> i) & 1) !== 0;
}

/** Alignment-pattern centre coordinates (ISO Annex E): first 6, last size − 7, even spacing. */
function alignmentPositions(version: number): number[] {
  if (version === 1) return [];
  const n = Math.floor(version / 7) + 2;
  const size = version * 4 + 17;
  const step = version === 32 ? 26 : Math.ceil((version * 4 + 4) / (n * 2 - 2)) * 2;
  const result = [6];
  for (let pos = size - 7; result.length < n; pos -= step) result.splice(1, 0, pos);
  return result;
}

function drawFinder(g: Grid, cx: number, cy: number): void {
  for (let dy = -4; dy <= 4; dy++) {
    for (let dx = -4; dx <= 4; dx++) {
      const x = cx + dx;
      const y = cy + dy;
      if (x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= g.size || y >= g.size) continue;
      const ring = Math.max(Math.abs(dx), Math.abs(dy));
      g.setFunction(x, y, ring !== 2 && ring !== 4); // ring 4 = separator
    }
  }
}

function drawAlignment(g: Grid, cx: number, cy: number): void {
  for (let dy = -2; dy <= 2; dy++) {
    for (let dx = -2; dx <= 2; dx++) {
      g.setFunction(cx + dx, cy + dy, Math.max(Math.abs(dx), Math.abs(dy)) !== 1);
    }
  }
}

/** 15-bit format information: (ECL, mask) + BCH(15,5) remainder, XOR 0x5412 (ISO 7.9). */
function formatBits(mask: number): number {
  const data = (ECL_BITS_L << 3) | mask;
  let rem = data;
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) rem = (rem << 1) ^ ((rem >>> 9) * 0x537);
  return ((data << 10) | (rem & 0x3ff)) ^ 0x5412;
}

function drawFormatBits(g: Grid, mask: number): void {
  const bits = formatBits(mask);
  const size = g.size;
  // Copy 1, around the top-left finder.
  for (let i = 0; i <= 5; i++) g.setFunction(8, i, bit(bits, i));
  g.setFunction(8, 7, bit(bits, 6));
  g.setFunction(8, 8, bit(bits, 7));
  g.setFunction(7, 8, bit(bits, 8));
  for (let i = 9; i < 15; i++) g.setFunction(14 - i, 8, bit(bits, i));
  // Copy 2, split between the top-right and bottom-left finders.
  for (let i = 0; i < 8; i++) g.setFunction(size - 1 - i, 8, bit(bits, i));
  for (let i = 8; i < 15; i++) g.setFunction(8, size - 15 + i, bit(bits, i));
  g.setFunction(8, size - 8, true); // the always-dark module
}

/** 18-bit version information: version + BCH(18,6) remainder (ISO 7.10), versions ≥ 7 only. */
function versionBits(version: number): number {
  let rem = version;
  for (let i = 0; i < 12; i++) rem = (rem << 1) ^ ((rem >>> 11) * 0x1f25);
  return (version << 12) | (rem & 0xfff);
}

function drawVersion(g: Grid, version: number): void {
  if (version < 7) return;
  const bits = versionBits(version);
  for (let i = 0; i < 18; i++) {
    const a = g.size - 11 + (i % 3);
    const b = Math.floor(i / 3);
    g.setFunction(a, b, bit(bits, i));
    g.setFunction(b, a, bit(bits, i));
  }
}

function drawFunctionPatterns(g: Grid, version: number): void {
  const size = g.size;
  for (let i = 0; i < size; i++) {
    g.setFunction(6, i, i % 2 === 0);
    g.setFunction(i, 6, i % 2 === 0);
  }
  drawFinder(g, 3, 3);
  drawFinder(g, size - 4, 3);
  drawFinder(g, 3, size - 4);
  const pos = alignmentPositions(version);
  const last = pos.length - 1;
  pos.forEach((y, i) => {
    pos.forEach((x, j) => {
      const overlapsFinder =
        (i === 0 && j === 0) || (i === 0 && j === last) || (i === last && j === 0);
      if (!overlapsFinder) drawAlignment(g, x, y);
    });
  });
  drawFormatBits(g, 0); // reserves the format area; redrawn once the mask is chosen
  drawVersion(g, version);
}

/** Places codeword bits in the two-module-wide zigzag from the bottom-right corner (ISO 7.7.3). */
function placeCodewords(g: Grid, codewords: Uint8Array): void {
  const size = g.size;
  const totalBits = codewords.length * 8;
  let i = 0;
  for (let right = size - 1; right >= 1; right -= 2) {
    if (right === 6) right = 5; // skip the vertical timing column
    const upward = ((right + 1) & 2) === 0;
    for (let vert = 0; vert < size; vert++) {
      const y = upward ? size - 1 - vert : vert;
      for (let j = 0; j < 2; j++) {
        const x = right - j;
        const index = y * size + x;
        if (g.isFunction[index] === 1) continue;
        if (i < totalBits) {
          g.modules[index] = ((codewords[i >>> 3] ?? 0) >>> (7 - (i & 7))) & 1;
          i++;
        } // else: remainder bit, left light (0) before masking
      }
    }
  }
  if (i !== totalBits) throw new Error(`QR placement mismatch: ${i} of ${totalBits} bits placed`);
}

function maskHit(mask: number, x: number, y: number): boolean {
  switch (mask) {
    case 0:
      return (x + y) % 2 === 0;
    case 1:
      return y % 2 === 0;
    case 2:
      return x % 3 === 0;
    case 3:
      return (x + y) % 3 === 0;
    case 4:
      return (Math.floor(x / 3) + Math.floor(y / 2)) % 2 === 0;
    case 5:
      return ((x * y) % 2) + ((x * y) % 3) === 0;
    case 6:
      return (((x * y) % 2) + ((x * y) % 3)) % 2 === 0;
    case 7:
      return (((x + y) % 2) + ((x * y) % 3)) % 2 === 0;
    default:
      throw new RangeError(`QR mask out of range: ${mask}`);
  }
}

/** XORs the mask into every non-function module; applying it twice restores the grid. */
function applyMask(g: Grid, mask: number): void {
  for (let y = 0; y < g.size; y++) {
    for (let x = 0; x < g.size; x++) {
      const index = y * g.size + x;
      if (g.isFunction[index] === 0 && maskHit(mask, x, y)) {
        g.modules[index] = (g.modules[index] ?? 0) ^ 1;
      }
    }
  }
}

// ───────────────────────────── Penalty (ISO 7.8.3) ─────────────────────────────

const N1 = 3;
const N2 = 3;
const N3 = 40;
const N4 = 10;
const FINDER_CORE = [1, 0, 1, 1, 1, 0, 1];

/** N1 (runs ≥ 5) and N3 (1:1:3:1:1 with 4 light modules before or after) along one line. */
function linePenalty(read: (i: number) => number, size: number): number {
  let score = 0;
  let runColor = -1;
  let runLength = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < size; i++) {
    const c = read(i);
    if (c === runColor) {
      runLength++;
    } else {
      if (runLength >= 5) score += N1 + (runLength - 5);
      runColor = c;
      runLength = 1;
    }
  }
  if (runLength >= 5) score += N1 + (runLength - 5);

  const lightAt = (i: number): boolean => i < 0 || i >= size || read(i) === 0; // quiet zone is light
  for (let p = 0; p + 7 <= size; p++) {
    let core = true;
    for (let k = 0; k < 7 && core; k++) core = read(p + k) === FINDER_CORE[k];
    if (!core) continue;
    let before = true;
    let after = true;
    for (let k = 1; k <= 4; k++) {
      before &&= lightAt(p - k);
      after &&= lightAt(p + 6 + k);
    }
    if (before || after) score += N3;
  }
  return score;
}

/** Total ISO penalty N1 + N2 + N3 + N4 of a finished symbol (lower is better). */
export function qrPenalty(m: QrMatrix): number {
  const { size, modules } = m;
  const at = (x: number, y: number): number => modules[y * size + x] ?? 0;
  let score = 0;
  for (let a = 0; a < size; a++) {
    score += linePenalty((i) => at(i, a), size); // row a
    score += linePenalty((i) => at(a, i), size); // column a
  }
  for (let y = 0; y + 1 < size; y++) {
    for (let x = 0; x + 1 < size; x++) {
      const c = at(x, y);
      if (c === at(x + 1, y) && c === at(x, y + 1) && c === at(x + 1, y + 1)) score += N2;
    }
  }
  let dark = 0;
  for (const v of modules) dark += v;
  const total = size * size;
  const k = Math.max(0, Math.ceil(Math.abs(dark * 20 - total * 10) / total) - 1);
  return score + k * N4;
}

// ───────────────────────────── Public API ─────────────────────────────

function utf8(text: string): Uint8Array {
  const bytes = new TextEncoder().encode(text);
  if (bytes.length > MAX_BYTES) {
    throw new RangeError(`QR payload is ${bytes.length} bytes; the maximum is ${MAX_BYTES}`);
  }
  return bytes;
}

function buildGrid(bytes: Uint8Array): Grid {
  let version = 1;
  while (qrByteCapacityL(version) < bytes.length || version === SKIPPED_VERSION) version++;
  const grid = new Grid(version * 4 + 17);
  drawFunctionPatterns(grid, version);
  placeCodewords(grid, interleaveWithEcc(encodeDataCodewords(bytes, version), version));
  return grid;
}

function finish(grid: Grid, mask: number): QrMatrix {
  applyMask(grid, mask);
  drawFormatBits(grid, mask);
  return { size: grid.size, modules: grid.modules };
}

/**
 * Encodes `text` (UTF-8, byte mode, ECC L) in the smallest version that fits (version 23 is
 * skipped, see SKIPPED_VERSION), choosing the lowest-penalty mask. Throws RangeError above
 * 2953 bytes.
 */
export function encodeQr(text: string): QrMatrix {
  const grid = buildGrid(utf8(text));
  let bestMask = 0;
  let bestScore = Infinity;
  for (let mask = 0; mask < 8; mask++) {
    applyMask(grid, mask);
    drawFormatBits(grid, mask);
    const score = qrPenalty({ size: grid.size, modules: grid.modules });
    if (score < bestScore) {
      bestScore = score;
      bestMask = mask;
    }
    applyMask(grid, mask); // undo
  }
  return finish(grid, bestMask);
}

/** Test hook: like encodeQr but with a forced mask (0–7). Not for application code. */
export function encodeQrWithMask(text: string, mask: number): QrMatrix {
  if (!Number.isInteger(mask) || mask < 0 || mask > 7) {
    throw new RangeError(`QR mask out of range: ${mask}`);
  }
  return finish(buildGrid(utf8(text)), mask);
}
```

Notes:
- Format bits: `(01 << 3 | mask)` with a BCH(15,5) remainder (generator `0x537`), XOR `0x5412`;
  version bits: BCH(18,6) remainder (generator `0x1F25`). Copy placement follows ISO 7.9/7.10.
- Penalty N3 counts each 1:1:3:1:1 core once when four light modules precede **or** follow it,
  treating the quiet zone outside the symbol as light; N4 = 10·k with
  k = ⌈|20·dark − 10·total| / total⌉ − 1 (clamped at 0).
- `placeCodewords` throws if the zigzag does not place exactly every codeword bit — an internal
  consistency check between the layout and the capacity formula.
