- [ ] **Step 17: Create `README.md`**

````markdown
# Tesbox

Tesbox is a free, self-hosted web app for a Tesla's built-in touchscreen browser. It synthesizes a
combustion-engine sound in real time from GPS speed, a real gearbox model and your own Tesla's
performance, and plays it through the cabin speakers. No accounts, no analytics: nothing leaves
the car.

> **Status: Phase 0 — in-car probe.** This build ships the probe page (`/probe/`) that measures
> what the car's browser can do (audio, output routes, steering-wheel events, GPS, storage) before
> the engine sound is built. The main page is a placeholder.

**Not affiliated with or endorsed by Tesla, Inc. or any car manufacturer.** Engine sounds are
synthesized impressions, not recordings of the named cars. Cabin sound only — keep it below
sirens, and never operate the touchscreen while driving.

## Run it

Tesbox is a static site in a public Docker image (`ghcr.io/atvriders/tesbox`, amd64 + arm64,
non-root nginx on port 8080).

```sh
docker compose up -d   # uses docker-compose.yml from this repo
```

You must serve it from **your own public HTTPS domain**, e.g. `https://tesbox.example.com`, via a
reverse proxy or tunnel that forwards to port 8080:

- geolocation and AudioWorklet only work in a secure (HTTPS) context;
- the car's browser cannot reach private LAN addresses.

The car needs Wi-Fi, a phone hotspot or Premium Connectivity for the first load.

## In the car (Phase 0)

1. Note the build: open `https://tesbox.example.com/version.json` and copy `sha`.
2. In the car's browser open `https://tesbox.example.com/probe/?b=<sha>` (the `b` parameter
   defeats the browser's long-lived HTML cache; keep the trailing slash after `probe`).
3. Follow [`docs/IN_CAR_CHECKLIST.md`](docs/IN_CAR_CHECKLIST.md): parked steps first, driving
   steps only by a passenger, then **Show QR** and scan the codes with a phone.

## Develop

Requires Node ≥ 22.12.

```sh
npm ci
npm run dev          # Vite dev server
npm test             # unit tests (Vitest)
npm run test:e2e     # Playwright: builds, runs `vite preview` with the production headers
npm run verify       # lint + typecheck + unit + build + e2e
npm run gen:anchor   # regenerate public/probe/anchor.wav (deterministic)
```

Header checks (`tests/e2e/headers.spec.ts`) run only against a container:
`E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 npx playwright test`.

## Releases (GitHub Actions)

Every push to `master` runs lint, typecheck, unit tests and build, then builds the image, runs it
and runs Playwright against it; only if everything is green it pushes
`ghcr.io/atvriders/tesbox:latest` and `:<commit sha>` for amd64 and arm64.

**Forks:** the first push creates a *private* GHCR package. Make it public once under the
package's settings and verify with an anonymous `docker pull`.

## Clean room and licence

Tesbox is implemented from papers, standards and first principles (plus one MIT-licensed
adaptation, the QR encoder, attributed in `NOTICES`); see
[`docs/CLEAN_ROOM.md`](docs/CLEAN_ROOM.md). MIT licence ([`LICENSE`](LICENSE)); attributions in
[`NOTICES`](NOTICES).
````

- [ ] **Step 18: Create `docs/IN_CAR_CHECKLIST.md`**

Parked (interactive) and driving (passenger-only / started while parked) steps; section 6 maps every spec §16 question to a probe action.

```markdown
# Tesbox in-car checklist (Phase 0 probe)

The probe page answers the open platform questions in spec §16 before any engine sound is built.
Results come back as QR codes (statistics only, never coordinates) and are committed as
`docs/research/probe-results-<date>.json` (an array: the interim export from step 3.2 plus the
final one from step 5.1).

**Safety rule:** the driver never touches the screen while the car moves. Every "Driving" step is
either started while parked (and then just runs) or done by a passenger. Interactive steps are
marked **Parked**.

## 0. Before you go

1. Deploy the image behind your public HTTPS domain or tunnel (spec §16 Q3; the domain stays out
   of the repo — this file uses the placeholder `https://tesbox.example.com`). The car's browser
   needs HTTPS for geolocation and AudioWorklet and cannot reach private LAN addresses.
2. Check the deployed build: open `https://tesbox.example.com/version.json` on a phone and note
   the `sha` (7 characters).
3. In the car, open `https://tesbox.example.com/probe/?b=<sha>` (the `b` parameter only defeats
   the browser's HTML cache). Use the trailing slash.
4. First load needs Wi-Fi, a phone hotspot or Premium Connectivity.
5. Have a phone ready to scan QR codes, and some music on a car source (radio, streaming, USB).
6. Start a GPX logger app on a phone mounted in the car and keep it recording through section 4
   (start it while parked). The GPX file is kept local and never committed (it has real
   coordinates); it is the input for Phase 1 gpx-to-replay and the `gpsLatencyS` refinement
   (section 7).

## 1. Parked — environment, build, storage

| Step | Probe action | Record |
|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | **Build** section shows "Up to date" | If "Stale build", tap the `?b=…` link and start again |
| 1.2 | **Environment**: read dpr, viewport, WebGL renderer, MCU guess, feature table | Automatic (in the QR) |
| 1.3 | **Storage** runs on load: note *Visits*, *Cache entry from earlier visit*, *Page controlled at load* | Automatic |
| 1.4 | **Export → Notes**: choose the car model | Model |

## 2. Parked — audio, routes, wheel, music

Repeat 2.2–2.8 for each route: `direct`, `media-element`, `direct + anchor` (2.2 stops the previous pass first, so each pass starts fresh and the *music* observation of 2.3 is recorded per route). Do 2.9 once, at the end of the last pass: the Notes select keeps a single answer, so note any per-route difference in the results commit.

| Step | Probe action | Record |
|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | Start your music on a car source | — |
| 2.2 | Pick the route, keep latencyHint `balanced`, tap **Start audio** (a low pulse plays). For the 2nd and 3rd route: if audio is still running, tap **Stop audio** first (Start stays disabled while audio runs), pick the route, then **Start audio** again, so the *music* question is answered for each route | Quanta counter rises; `perfInWorklet` in the readout |
| 2.3 | Did your music pause or keep playing underneath? | Observation *music*: paused / mixed |
| 2.3b | Tap **Pitch step** a few times (the pulse jumps 55 ↔ 110 Hz) and judge the delay between each tap and the pitch change on this route. Compare the three routes: `media-element` adds `<audio>` buffering that `outputLatency` does not show | Delay none / slight / obvious per route, noted in the results commit (the context latencies are kept automatically as QR note `latency.<route>`) |
| 2.4 | Minimise the browser (full-screen map or another app) for ~20 s | — |
| 2.5 | While minimised: is a Tesbox Miniplayer card visible? Does the pulse keep playing? | *miniplayer*, *survivesMinimise* |
| 2.6 | Return to the browser | *Hidden clock ratio* fills in automatically |
| 2.7 | Tilt the LEFT scroll wheel right, then left (tilt only — do not press) | *wheelTilt* = yes if the event log shows `nexttrack`/`previoustrack` or a `MediaTrack…` key |
| 2.8 | If audio stopped: tap **Resume**; if that fails, **Recreate** | Recovery counters (automatic) |
| 2.9 | Last pass only: tap **Stop audio**: does your music come back by itself? | Notes → *Music resumes after Stop audio* (one answer; per-route differences seen at earlier stops go in the results commit) |
| 2.10 | With navigation to a destination running on the map: **Start audio**, then **Benchmark** (about 15 s) | Headroom k (automatic; kept as QR note `bench.balanced`) |
| 2.11 | Intel MCU only: **Stop audio**, select latencyHint `playback`, **Start audio**, **Benchmark** again | outputLatency + headroom for the A/B (automatic; QR note `bench.playback`) |
| 2.12 | Model S/X only: listen for pumping or cancellation of the pulse (Active Road Noise Reduction) | Notes → *Road-noise cancellation interferes* |
| 2.13 | Optional: press steering-wheel buttons, press a paired gamepad's buttons | Logged automatically |

## 3. Parked — motion baseline

| Step | Probe action | Record |
|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | Tap **Probe motion (5 s)** with the car stationary | events, rate, range (automatic; every run is kept as QR note `motion.<n>`) |
| 3.2 | **Interim export:** tap **Show QR** and scan every page (**Next QR**) now, before driving | Parked results live only in the page's memory; a crash or reload while driving (section 4) wipes them |

## 4. Driving — passenger operates, driver only drives

If there is no passenger: start one GPS run while parked using a longer run, e.g.
`https://tesbox.example.com/probe/?b=<sha>&gpsSeconds=300`, then drive; do the other modes on
later drives. Never tap the screen while moving.

| Step | Probe action | Record |
|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | **GPS**: **Run 60 s** for `poll-hiacc-off`, then `poll-hiacc-on`, `watch-hiacc-off`, `watch-hiacc-on` (one at a time, on an open road with some stops) | Summary table rows (automatic) |
| 4.2 | During one run, minimise the browser for ~15 s, then return | *hidden fixes* column (expected 0) |
| 4.3 | If the page reloads or crashes during a run, reopen it | Crash banner + *crashed* column (automatic). Rows of runs finished before the crash are gone too: repeat those modes (or, passenger, take another interim **Show QR** after each finished run) |
| 4.4 | Passenger: **Probe motion (5 s)** while the car accelerates moderately | events, rate, range |
| 4.5 | Keep audio running on the best route during the drive and note any dropouts | Event log (automatic) |

## 5. After the drive — export

| Step | Probe action | Record |
|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | Parked: tap **Show QR**; scan every page (**Next QR**) with the phone, in order | Each code starts `TBX i/n` |
| 5.2 | Reassemble each export separately (interim from 3.2 and final): strip the first line `TBX i/n` from each scan and concatenate in order | One JSON document per export |
| 5.3 | Commit both as `docs/research/probe-results-<date>.json` (an array of the exports; no coordinates) | — |
| 5.4 | Next day (after the car slept): open the probe again | *Visits* +1, *Cache entry age* (shown in seconds; 3600 s = 1 h) roughly equals the time since the previous visit, *Page controlled at load* = true means persistence works |

## 6. Spec §16 questions → probe actions

| § 16 | Question | Answered by |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Approve the per-phase commit + push exception? | Not a probe question — answered by the user in conversation |
| Q2 | Reference clips: supplied, or fetched locally? | Not a probe question — answered by the user in conversation |
| Q3 | Which public HTTPS domain/tunnel hosts the probe? | Step 0.1 (kept out of the repo) |
| Q4 | Does left-wheel tilt reach `nexttrack`/`previoustrack` or `MediaTrack*` keys, per route/model/firmware? | Steps 2.7, 1.4 (model); firmware noted in the results commit message |
| Q5 | Which route keeps audio alive and shows the Miniplayer when minimised? Do fixes arrive while minimised? | Steps 2.4–2.6 per route; step 4.2 (*hidden fixes*) |
| Q6 | GPS cadence, fix age (measured delivery delay; provisional `gpsLatencyS` recorded per section 7), `coords.speed` null/zero rate, `watchPosition`/high-accuracy stability, altitude | Steps 4.1, 4.3 (interval, age, speed null/zero, zero-while-moving, accuracy, altitude, errors, crashes); step 0.6 (phone GPX log for the Phase 1 `gpsLatencyS` refinement) |
| Q7 | `performance.now` in the worklet; k× headroom with the map running; Intel `latencyHint` A/B | Steps 2.2 (`perfInWorklet`), 2.10, 2.11 |
| Q8 | Service Worker + Cache Storage available and persistent across drives/sleep? | Steps 1.3, 5.4 |
| Q9 | Does Tesbox replace or mix with the user's music? | Steps 2.3, 2.9 |
| Q10 | Is DeviceMotion exposed with plausible longitudinal acceleration? (never DeviceOrientation) | Steps 3.1, 4.4 |
| Q11 | (S/X only) Any interaction with Active Road Noise Reduction? | Step 2.12 |

## 7. Defaults recorded from the results (spec §15 Phase 0 exit gate)

After the results are committed, record in the spec: default output route, GPS method,
high-accuracy flag, Intel `latencyHint`, the median fix age (`ageMedianMs`) as the measured
delivery delay, and a provisional `gpsLatencyS` (keep the spec default 1.0 s unless the data show
otherwise; do not set it to the median age, because `ageS` already adds `Date.now() − timestamp`,
spec §4.3). Phase 1 refines `gpsLatencyS` against the phone GPX log from step 0.6.
```

- [ ] **Step 19: Create `docs/CLEAN_ROOM.md`**

The US 6,959,094 status was read from Google Patents during planning (2026-10-06): "Expired – Lifetime", adjusted expiration 2022-12-26.

```markdown
# Clean-room log

Tesbox is an original implementation of a publicly known product concept (GPS-driven engine
sound in the Tesla browser). This file is the binding policy (spec §2) plus the source log for
every algorithm in the repository. Add a row whenever an algorithm lands.

## Policy

- Nothing from rupteur.app — code, engine definitions, calibration data, audio, copy, branding or
  UI assets — is fetched, read or used. The one download made during discovery was deleted unread.
- `github.com/UnendlessGit/juniper-sound-lab`, and any repo or app that references rupteur.app or
  appears derived from it, is never opened or read.
- Algorithms are implemented from papers, standards and first principles only, except MIT code adapted with attribution as allowed below.
- No code is copied from GPL sources (e.g. SDT `SDTMotor.h` — not even read), non-commercial
  sources (PTR code/dataset, PolyForm-NC projects such as sedicivalvole) or unlicensed sources
  (Farnell's Pd patches). MIT code may be adapted with attribution in `NOTICES` (so far: Project Nayuki's QR Code generator library → `src/platform/qr.ts`, MIT notice in `NOTICES`).
- Engine and Tesla data are compiled per vehicle from primary/public documents with per-field-group
  `sources`; no third-party specs database is bulk-extracted.
- Third-party runtime code shipped to the browser: only the MIT-attributed QR encoder adaptation in `src/platform/qr.ts`. Test-only tools are listed in `NOTICES`.

## Phase 0 source log

| Module | Algorithm | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `src/platform/qr.ts` | QR Code encoder: byte mode, ECC level L, versions 1–40, Reed–Solomon over GF(2⁸) (primitive polynomial 0x11D), BCH format/version information, 8 mask patterns, penalty rules N1–N4 | ISO/IEC 18004:2015 *QR Code bar code symbology specification*; code structure adapted from Project Nayuki, *QR Code generator library* (MIT) |
| `src/platform/geo.ts` | Great-circle distance (haversine), mean Earth radius R = 6 371 008.8 m | R. W. Sinnott, "Virtues of the Haversine", *Sky & Telescope* 68(2), 1984; H. Moritz, "Geodetic Reference System 1980", *Bulletin Géodésique* 54, 1980 (mean radius R₁) |
| `src/probe/gps-stats.ts` | Nearest-rank median / p95 | R. J. Hyndman & Y. Fan, "Sample Quantiles in Statistical Packages", *The American Statistician* 50(4), 1996 (inverse empirical CDF) |
| `src/probe/gps.ts` | `getCurrentPosition` chain / `watchPosition` runner | W3C *Geolocation* Recommendation |
| `src/probe/work.ts` | Synthetic multiply-add load kernel (k × 400 ops per sample) | First principles |
| `src/probe/audio-worklet.ts` | Raised-cosine pulse train; one-pole low-pass y[n] = (1 − a)·x[n] + a·y[n−1], a = e^(−2π·f_c/f_s) | First principles; W3C *Web Audio API* (AudioWorklet) |
| `src/probe/drift.ts` | Audio-clock vs wall-clock ratio from `getOutputTimestamp()` | W3C *Web Audio API* |
| `src/probe/audio.ts`, `src/probe/inputs.ts` | Output routes, Media Session handlers, input logging, DeviceMotion | W3C *Web Audio API*, *Media Session*, *DeviceOrientation Event* (DeviceMotion only), *Gamepad*, *UI Events* |
| `src/probe/storage-probe.ts`, `public/probe/sw.js` | Cache Storage round-trip, service-worker registration | W3C *Service Workers* |
| `tools/make-anchor-wav.ts` | RIFF/WAVE PCM writer; xorshift32 PRNG; triangular (TPDF) noise scaled to −60 dBFS RMS | Microsoft & IBM, *Multimedia Programming Interface and Data Specifications 1.0*, 1991 (RIFF WAVE); G. Marsaglia, "Xorshift RNGs", *Journal of Statistical Software* 8(14), 2003 |

## Planned sources (later phases, methods only)

| Area | Source |
|---|---|
| Engine synthesis (DspCore) | S. Baldan, H. Lachambre, S. Delle Monache, P. Boussard, "Physically informed car engine sound synthesis for virtual and augmented environments", SIVE 2015 |
| Engine synthesis methods | A. Farnell, *Designing Sound*, MIT Press 2010 (text only; the Pd patches are not used) |
| Pulse shaping / regime gating | Doerfler & Wyse, PTR, arXiv 2603.07584 / 2603.09391 (paper only; CC BY-NC code and dataset not used) |
| Cycle-to-cycle variation | Bodden & Belschner, Inter-noise 2014 |
| Virtual bass | E. Larsen & R. M. Aarts, "Reproducing low-pitched signals through small loudspeakers", *JAES* 2002 |
| Vehicle model | Standard vehicle dynamics; EPA Test Car List (Target road-load coefficients, N/V); Tesla owner's manuals |

## Patents checked (not legal advice)

| Patent | Scope | Status for Tesbox |
|---|---|---|
| US 6,959,094 (Cascone et al., physical-model ICE sound synthesis; filed 2001-04-20) | Synthesis of internal-combustion engine vehicle sounds | Google Patents legal status "Expired – Lifetime", adjusted expiration 2022-12-26 (checked 2026-10-06, patents.google.com/patent/US6959094B1) |
| Harman US 12,243,510; US 9,553,553 | Sample/PSOLA and harmonic+residual analysis of recordings | Not used: Tesbox has no recordings or sample banks |
| Hyundai/Kia US 11,904,769 | User-customised virtual engine sound with emotion/review models | Not used: no such models |
| MaxxBass US 5,930,373 | Virtual bass | Expired; Tesbox uses only the basic Larsen & Aarts harmonic method |
```

- [ ] **Step 20: Create `.github/workflows/ci.yml`**

```yaml
name: CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [master]
  pull_request:

permissions:
  contents: read
  packages: write

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: 22
          cache: npm
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm run lint
      - run: npm run typecheck
      - run: npm test
      - run: npm run build

  e2e:
    needs: test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: 22
          cache: npm
      - run: npm ci
      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3
      - name: Build amd64 image (loaded into the local daemon)
        uses: docker/build-push-action@v6
        with:
          context: .
          platforms: linux/amd64
          load: true
          tags: tesbox:ci
          build-args: GITHUB_SHA=${{ github.sha }}
          cache-from: type=gha
          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max
      - name: Start container
        run: docker run -d --name tesbox -p 8080:8080 tesbox:ci
      - name: Wait for /version.json
        run: |
          for i in $(seq 1 30); do
            if curl -fsS http://localhost:8080/version.json; then exit 0; fi
            sleep 1
          done
          docker logs tesbox
          exit 1
      - name: Served build SHA matches this commit
        run: test "$(curl -fsS http://localhost:8080/version.json | jq -r .sha)" = "${GITHUB_SHA::7}"
      - name: Cache Playwright browsers
        uses: actions/cache@v4
        with:
          path: ~/.cache/ms-playwright
          key: playwright-${{ runner.os }}-${{ hashFiles('package-lock.json') }}
      - run: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium
      - name: Playwright against the container
        run: npx playwright test
        env:
          E2E_BASE_URL: http://localhost:8080
      - name: Upload Playwright report
        if: failure()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: playwright-report
          path: |
            playwright-report/
            test-results/
          retention-days: 7
      - name: Container logs
        if: failure()
        run: docker logs tesbox

  publish:
    needs: [test, e2e]
    if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/master'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: docker/setup-qemu-action@v3
      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3
      - uses: docker/login-action@v3
        with:
          registry: ghcr.io
          username: ${{ github.actor }}
          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
      - name: Build and push multi-arch image
        uses: docker/build-push-action@v6
        with:
          context: .
          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64
          push: true
          build-args: GITHUB_SHA=${{ github.sha }}
          tags: |
            ghcr.io/atvriders/tesbox:latest
            ghcr.io/atvriders/tesbox:${{ github.sha }}
          cache-from: type=gha
          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max
```

- [ ] **Step 21: Ship `NOTICES` and `LICENSE` in the Docker image**

MIT requires the notice in "all copies or substantial portions": the published image (and the bundle every browser loads from it) contains the adapted QR encoder, but T11's runtime stage copies only `dist/`. In `Dockerfile`, directly after the line `COPY --from=build /app/dist /usr/share/nginx/html`, insert:

```dockerfile
# MIT notice for the adapted QR encoder (src/platform/qr.ts) ships with the bundle.
COPY NOTICES /usr/share/nginx/html/NOTICES.txt
COPY LICENSE /usr/share/nginx/html/LICENSE.txt
```

Nothing else in the Dockerfile changes. nginx serves both through its `location /` block (`no-cache`, `text/plain` from the stock `mime.types`, with the security headers). This belongs here, not in T11, because this task creates both files; T11's optional Docker build (its Step 17) therefore never hits a missing file.

- [ ] **Step 22: Run the repo-files test and make sure Playwright output is ignored by git**

Run: `npx vitest run tests/unit/repo-files.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, `Tests  11 passed (11)`.
Then run: `for p in node_modules/ dist/ test-results/ playwright-report/; do grep -qxF "$p" .gitignore || echo "$p" >> .gitignore; done; git status --short --ignored test-results playwright-report`
Expected: `.gitignore` gains only lines that were missing (none, if T1 added them all); the `git status` line shows `!! test-results/` (ignored) and nothing untracked from Playwright.

- [ ] **Step 23: Lint and typecheck**

Run: `npm run lint && npm run typecheck`
Expected: both exit 0 with no diagnostics.

- [ ] **Step 24: Full Phase 0 verification**

Run: `npm run verify && grep -l '@license MIT' dist/assets/probe-*.js`
Expected, in order: ESLint clean; `tsc` clean; Vitest — every file under `tests/unit/` passes (this author's files alone: storage-probe 17, make-anchor-wav 7, probe-page 7, csp 9, headers-plugin 8, repo-files 11); `vite build` lists `dist/index.html`, `dist/probe/index.html`, `dist/version.json` and the hashed assets (including `audio-worklet-<hash>.js`); Playwright — `9 passed`, `4 skipped` (the headers spec runs only against the container); finally `grep -l` prints `dist/assets/probe-<hash>.js` (T3's MIT attribution survived minification). The phase is ready for its single verified commit only when this command exits 0.
